Changelog

What changed, with screenshots

For each release: what it did before, what it does now, pictures of both, and how it was checked, including what was not. Newest first. Plain release notes are on GitHub.

seen on screenpartly checkedgap found

Hopper 0.88.0

19 Nov 2026

Server tab card: state pill, three tiles for players, TPS and ping, labelled Stop and Restart

Apps
Hopper desktop. Hopper Server's web page already has this look on its server page (a state chip in the header, labelled Start, Stop and Restart, a More menu), so it was left alone. There is no Hopper Server release this time.
Before
The card on an instance's Server tab showed the name and a small coloured "running" next to it, then a thin row of signal bars, ping, players and a TPS badge. Stop and Restart were bare icons, and a refresh icon ("Compare again") sat in the card header.
After
The header is a dot, the name and a state pill: Running, Stopped, Starting, Stopping, Restarting, Crashed, Sleeping, Couldn't wake or Unreachable. While the server runs there are three tiles: Players, TPS (green, yellow or red) and Ping. When it is stopped the card says "Start it to see players, TPS and ping." The buttons read Stop, Restart and Start; Console and the ... menu stay as icons on the right. In a narrow side panel Stop and Restart step down to icons with tooltips, and under about 17 rem the tiles become one block with a line per figure. "Compare again" moved to the comparison toolbar, a refresh icon next to "Sync all...".
Good to know
The Stopping state has no Stop button, same as before: the server is already shutting down. TPS can keep showing for about two minutes after a server stops being asked (older behaviour), but the card hides the tiles unless the server is running.
  • src/gpui/widgets.rs
  • src/gpui/server_control.rs
  • src/gpui/rigsync.rs
seen on screen
  • A test build on a test PC with made-up servers (a fake game that answers like a real one) behind a throwaway Hopper Server, and a made-up instance. The tests started, stopped and crashed the fake servers, put 100 players on one, slowed its ticks, and shut the panel down. The old and the new build were both looked at in dark; the new one also in light and at the narrowest window.
  • Measured on the screenshots (1 pt = 1.75 px): the three tiles are 116 px wide each with 13 px gaps, in dark and light, and sit 20 px from each card edge. With a 31-letter name at the narrowest window the pill is 23 px clear of the name and 20 px from the card edge. At the narrowest window the buttons stay on one row (30 px spare on the right). The grey labels read at 5.48 contrast in dark and 5.25 in light; the TPS numbers at 4.8 to 10.0 (green, yellow and red, dark and light).
  • Tests: the 143 desktop tests pass, including 3 new ones for the pill words.
partly checked
  • Not seen on screen: the Sleeping and Couldn't wake pills (only covered by automatic tests; the test setup has no sleeping server), the "Control from Hopper..." state, and how the Compare again button looks while it loads or is disabled (the icon was only seen idle).
  • Not measured: the card's padding next to the Loader and Server pack cards (they only show for servers built from a CurseForge pack, and the test setup has none; all three share the same card code), ping and TPS tile alignment at 150 % and 200 % zoom, the keyboard focus ring on the card buttons, and Hopper Server's web page (not changed). Pill words fit in every state; that was seen, not measured.
gap found
  • "Can't reach Hopper Server" (the Unreachable state) runs about 12 px into the card's right padding at the narrowest window. The text is the same as before this change, and it is left as a follow-up.

Friends: shared settings show the summary card (Manage, Stop all) from 3 shared mods instead of 5

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server's web page has no Friends panel, so there is nothing to match there.
Before
With 4 or fewer shared mods the Friends panel kept one row per mod, so 3 or 4 mods already took up a lot of room. The summary card from 0.85.0 only started at 5.
After
The summary card (overlapping mod icons, how many settings changed, Manage... and Stop all) takes over from 3 shared mods. With 1 or 2 shared mods the rows stay as they were.
  • src/gpui/shared_settings.rs
not seen on screen at 3 and 4 mods
  • Not seen on screen: the card with 3 or 4 shared mods. That needs a local mailbox and shared mods, which this check did not set up. Read from the code only: the card is the same layout at any count, shows no "+N more" up to 6 icons, and draws 3 or 4 icons for 3 or 4 mods.
  • Tests: the automatic test that fixes the starting count was renamed and now expects 3; the 143 desktop tests pass.

Hopper 0.87.1 · Hopper Server 0.79.4

18 Nov 2026

Mod page: copy the name, and close from the slim header

Apps
Hopper desktop (seen on screen). Hopper Server got the matching close button on project pages opened beside a list, see the last card.
Before
On a mod's page the name could not be selected. The slim header that replaces the big one when you scroll had no close button, so you had to scroll back up to close the page.
After
The name can be selected and copied (drag over it, then Ctrl+C), in the big header and in the slim one that shows when you scroll. The slim header now has the close button too, next to Update or Install, so you can close the page from either header. This covers every page opened beside a list (mods, packs, shaders, data packs, servers, backups).
Good to know
The author link ("by ...") stays a link, so it cannot be selected. In the narrowest side panel the slim header's row of tabs cuts off the last tab (Issues shows as a sliver). It was already like that before this change and is a follow-up.
  • src/gpui/browse.rs
  • src/gpui/widgets.rs
seen on screen
  • A test build on a test PC with a made-up instance (not a real pack), in dark and light. Dragging over the name in the big and the slim header selects it without opening anything or closing the page. Ctrl+C put the name on the clipboard (Occultism). Clicking the close button in the slim header closes the page.
  • Measured on the screenshots (1 pt = 1.75 px): in the narrowest side panel the slim header's close button and the big header's one have the same size (39 x 39 px hover box) and sit the same distance from the window edge (33 px). The X glyph is 13 x 13 px, 46 px from the window edge, in every dark and light shot. A 40-letter name (Applied Energistics 2 Wireless Terminals) is cut with dots first: it ends 19 px before the Install button, which keeps its full width (170 px), with 17 px to the close button.
partly checked
  • Not checked: the keyboard focus ring on the new close button (it is the same button as the big header's, which has one, but it was not looked at), the page title of an author page (same code, not dragged over), pages from Modrinth or GitHub, and windows narrower than the narrowest side panel.
  • No light-theme picture from before the change exists, so light has no before and after pair. The light close button's hover colour was too faint to measure; its position was measured instead.

Hopper Server: project pages opened beside a list can be closed from the slim header

Apps
Hopper Server (web page).
Before
When a project page was opened beside a list, its close or back button was only in the top row, which scrolled out of reach.
After
The slim header has a close button (or a back arrow when it goes back one step) too.
no card
  • No change card of its own: it was built but not seen in a browser, so how it looks is not checked.

Hopper 0.87.0 · Hopper Server 0.79.3

18 Nov 2026

Instance page: see how many players a paired server has

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server's web page already shows players online, so there is nothing to match there.
Before
An instance paired with a server showed its state, ping and TPS in the Server button at the top and on the Server tab, but the number of players was only in the button's tooltip.
After
The Server button now shows the players online after the server name (like 12/200), and the Server tab shows it between ping and TPS. It shows only while the server runs and has a player limit. When the window gets tight the count is the first thing to go, so it never folds Folder or Settings into the menu sooner than before (the tooltip still has it).
  • crates/hopper-core/src/panel.rs
  • src/gpui/widgets.rs
  • src/gpui/rigsync.rs
  • src/gpui/library.rs
seen on screen
  • A test build on a test PC with a made-up instance paired to a throwaway Hopper Server (with a fake game that reports joins, leaves and TPS), in dark and light, at 12/200, 100/100 and 0/16, and with a 30-letter server name. Narrower windows (1300 and 960 wide) were checked too: the count drops out first and the rest folds the same as before.
  • Measured on the screenshots: the Server button's content has equal padding (9 px left, 9 to 10 px right). The count reads at 5.81 to 1 (dark) and 5.84 to 1 (light) on the selected button. On the Server tab the count sits on the same line as the ping text (0 px off) and reads at 6.35 to 1 (dark) and 5.89 to 1 (light). The worst case, 100/100 plus the TPS chip, fits on one line with 25 px to spare, and the card keeps its height.
  • The long name is cut with dots before the count is touched; nothing wraps or overlaps.
partly checked
  • Not seen on screen: a stopped server (the test server restarted at once; the hidden state is only covered by an automatic test), a server that is starting, and an instance with 3 or more paired servers.
  • Not measured: counts with 4 digits (like 1000/1000), other zoom levels, or other themes than the default dark and light. A before picture exists but is not shown here (it has no count to compare).

A missing required mod shows its real name and icon, and opens its page

Apps
Both. The lookup is shared. The desktop notice is what changed on screen; Hopper Server has no such notice but its crash card uses the same lookup.
Before
A required mod that was missing showed its id in the notice (arcane_framework). Mods whose page title has a subtitle, like Arcane Framework, were not found: clicking opened an empty Browse search with 0 results.
After
The notice shows the mod's real name and icon (Arcane Framework - Library for the Almanac Mods), and clicking it opens its page beside the list. When nothing is found, Browse now searches the id as words (like "arcane framework") instead of the raw id.
Good to know
When a mod is not found it can ask CurseForge up to two more times before the normal search.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/install.rs
  • src/gpui/addons_extra.rs
seen on screen
  • A test build on a test PC with a made-up instance whose mod needs Arcane Framework: the notice and the click, before and after. Also a made-up id that exists nowhere, which opens Browse with its words. Dark theme only.
partly checked
  • Not tested: the Install button actually downloading the mod (it was not clicked), and the light theme.
  • Not measured: there is no layout change (only the text and icon in the existing notice), so no pixel measurement was made.

Hopper Server: installing a mod from a crash report finds mods with a subtitle in their title

Apps
Hopper Server (web page).
Before
Installing a mod that a crash report says is missing could not find mods whose page title has a subtitle (like Arcane Framework).
After
It finds them now, using the same lookup as the desktop notice.
no card
  • No change card of its own: it was not seen on screen. The text comes from the release notes.

Hopper 0.86.0 · Hopper Server 0.79.2

18 Nov 2026

Mods from GitHub and other places now show if they are client-only or server-only

Apps
Both. Hopper reads the answer from the mod file itself; the desktop Mods tab shows it, and Hopper Server's web page shows the same words.
Before
A mod that is not on CurseForge or Modrinth (a GitHub mod, for example) showed a "?" for its side and had no chip. Server sync did not know it was client-only.
After
Hopper reads the side from the mod file when neither site says. The Mods tab shows the "Client only" chip, and the tooltip says "Client only (from the jar)". Server sync keeps those client-only mods off the server like any other client-only mod; "Sync anyway" still sends one. If a mod says different things for different parts, or says nothing, Hopper does not guess and keeps the "?".
Good to know
CurseForge and Modrinth are still asked first; the file is only opened when they have nothing. A GitHub Fabric mod whose file says it runs on "*" (any side) still shows "Client + server". That comes from older code and is a follow-up.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/deps.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/modsides.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/serverpack.rs
  • src/gpui/widgets.rs
  • src/gpui/addons.rs
  • src/gpui/rigsync.rs
seen on screen
  • A test build on a test PC with a made-up instance, the real Mob Map Icons file from GitHub, in dark and light: the Mods tab rows, the Client only chip, its tooltip, and the Side column hover card. Tooltip text reads at 15.3 to 16.5 contrast and is not clipped in either theme.
  • A mod with a Fabric "client" file shows Client only, a mod with mixed sides shows no chip and a "?", and a mod CurseForge tags as both stays Client + server (CurseForge wins).
partly checked
  • Not seen on screen: the server sync dialog (only covered by an automatic test that a client-only file stays off the server), and Hopper Server's web page.
  • Not measured: the tooltip box padding, the narrowest window and the longest text. No before pictures: the old build was not to hand.
gap found
  • A GitHub Fabric mod with environment "*" still shows "Client + server". Older code copies that answer in before the new check runs. Follow-up, not done.

Hopper Server: a mod's side from the mod file, marked "(from the jar)"

Apps
Hopper Server (web page).
Before
A mod that is not on CurseForge or Modrinth showed no side in the Mods list.
After
The side read from the mod file shows in the Mods list and on the mod's page, marked "(from the jar)".
no card
  • No change card of its own: this is web page text only, and it was not seen on screen. The text comes from the release notes.

Hopper 0.85.0 · Hopper Server 0.79.1

18 Nov 2026

Friends panel: many shared settings fit in one small card, with a Manage window

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server's web page has no Friends panel, so there is nothing to match there.
Before
Every mod that shared its settings with a group got its own row with a Stop button, so 20 mods pushed People out of view.
After
With 4 or fewer shared mods the rows stay as they were. With 5 or more, one small card shows overlapping mod icons, how many more there are, how many settings changed ("43 settings changed. Your friends get them right away."), and two buttons: Manage... and Stop all. Manage... opens a window where you can search the shared mods, filter by All, Changed or No changes yet, and tick mods to stop sharing several at once (drag across rows, or Shift-click for a range). Clicking a mod's name there opens its page beside the window, and the window stays open.
Good to know
Typing in the search box or changing the filter drops ticks on rows that are now hidden, so Stop sharing selected only acts on rows you can see. Stop all asks first.
  • src/gpui/shared_settings.rs (new)
  • src/gpui/friends.rs
  • src/gpui/main.rs
seen on screen
  • A test build on a test PC with a made-up group, made-up mods and a local mailbox, in dark and light and at the narrowest window: 3 mods (rows), 14 mods (card), the Manage window, drag and Shift-click ticking, the Changed filter, a search with no match, a mod's page beside the window, Stop sharing selected, and the Stop all confirmation.
  • Measured on screen: with 14 mods the People list ends 499 px above the bottom of a 900 pt window, so it stays in view. The mod icons end 22 px before the "+8 more" label. The Manage footer sits 20 px from each edge (15 px at the narrowest window). Table rows are all 34 pt apart. The helper text and buttons read at 5.2 to 6.9 contrast in dark and light.
partly checked
  • Not checked: Ctrl-click ranges (only Shift was tried), the real friends service (the app talked to a local mailbox), using the window with the keyboard only, and Hopper Server.
  • Not measured: a 1080p window height, a 20-mod pack on screen, hover colours and the keyboard focus ring on the new buttons. A long mod name is cut with "..." before the Changed column; seen, not measured.
  • No before pictures for the card: the old build was not kept. The old layout is the 3-mod rows, unchanged.
  • The test also looked up a made-up mod id on CurseForge when a name was clicked (a read only), the one outside request it made.

Relay through a VPS: bigger groups on one mobile network can join

Apps
Hopper Server (the relay).
Before
Only 8 players at once could join through the relay from the same network block, so a big group on one mobile carrier was turned away.
After
Up to 16 players at once can join from the same network block.
no card
  • No change card was written for this change, so there are no screenshots and no test notes here. The text comes from the release notes.

Hopper 0.84.0 · Hopper Server 0.79.0

18 Nov 2026

Friends groups: take changes only from the group's maker, and send yours when you press Push

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server has no friends groups, so there is nothing to match there.
Before
Every member's change to a group pack came into your copy by itself, and every change you made went out within a second or two.
After
Each group on this PC has Take changes from: Everyone (the default) or Only <maker>. With Only, other people's changes wait, and the pack page and the Friends panel say whose changes wait and how many, with a Pull from <name> button for each person. Settings › Friends has Share my changes, also per PC: Right away (the default) or When I press Push. With Push, your changes stay on this PC until you press Push 3 changes on the pack page or the group row, or Push all in the Friends panel.
Good to know
A group row in the Friends panel shows a pull button for the first person waiting; the pack page shows up to three. A pack you have just shared goes out right away even with Push by hand, because sharing is your own decision. A single friend who is not in a group gets only the Friends panel's Push bar, with no button on their row. In a narrow window the strip folds to one button and a menu.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/syncmode.rs (new)
  • crates/hopper-core/src/groupstatus.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/settings.rs
  • src/gpui/store/sync_modes.rs (new)
  • src/gpui/store/friends.rs
  • src/gpui/group_pack.rs
  • src/gpui/friends.rs
  • src/gpui/settings_page.rs
  • src/gpui/app.rs
seen on screen
  • A test build with a made-up group over two empty test instances, no relay needed: only Mia's changes taken, the same with the group open in the Friends panel in dark and light, a pull from Noah (2 changes in, Ava's still waiting), switching back to Everyone (Ava's change came in), one person waiting, the narrow window, the narrow window's menu, changes held back for Push, and Settings › Friends.
  • Measured on screen: Pull from Noah is centred in its button (13 px on each side), Push 3 changes is centred side to side (13 and 12 px), and nothing is clipped or wrapped at 960 by 600 with the side panel open. The scratch copy no longer reaches the friends service: its data folder holds no mailbox or identity file.
  • Tests passed: 8 new ones for the change, plus 4 for the group status and 2 for settings.
partly checked
  • Not checked: A real two-PC group through the relay. Only the made-up group was used; nothing was sent for real.
  • Restart while changes are held. Covered by a unit test only (held_back_changes_are_found_again_after_a_restart).
  • Friend shares that aren't in a group: their Push lives only in the Friends panel bar, and it wasn't seen on screen.
  • The "Change in Settings" button's jump.
  • Not measured: colour contrast of the new chips; they use the shared chip colours that already exist.
  • No before pictures: these states did not exist before this change.

Mod names in notices open their page; the author in the Mods list opens a peek

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server's web page was left alone (see below).
Before
"1 required mod is missing" listed bare ids ("arcane_framework (for Spell Atlas)"); mod names in other notices were plain text; and the author in the Mods list was plain text.
After
Every mod a notice names shows its icon and real name, and opens its page beside the view. The author in the Mods list is a link that opens a side peek with that author's projects.
Good to know
Where the strip has no room (a narrow window, under 46 rem) the names open under Details, and the old plain tooltip stays. The crash and stuck-start notices use the same line, but those two were not seen on screen.

  • src/gpui/addons_extra.rs

seen on screen
  • A test build with throwaway instances of mods from Modrinth only, clicked through in the app: the missing-mod strip, opening a required mod's page beside the Mods tab, the Mods list with its Author column, the author peek, the group strip naming a missing mod and its click-through, the strip in a light 960 by 600 window, and beside a side panel.
  • Measured on screen: the names read at 5.3 to 5.65 to 1 in the light theme and 6.07 to 1 on dark, and a mod icon is 18.5 pt. The narrow details row keeps one line without clipping. A mod icon's centre sits 2.5 to 3 px above the middle of the letters, the same wherever the icon is drawn; that was left as it is.
  • Tests passed: 14 for the shared list of what a mod needs, 2 of them new, 3 more new ones for the notices, and every changed test program.
partly checked
  • Not checked: server crash and stuck-start notices on screen (the line is shared with the strip, built but not seen); the duplicate-mods strip on screen; Hopper Server's web page; zoom other than 100.
  • Not measured: pixel gaps of the author cell; dark 960 contrast; hover colours.
  • No before picture: the maintainer's report screenshot is the old look, the plain strip with bare ids.

Hopper Server's Mods list: the author opens a side panel with their projects

Apps
Hopper Server's web page only. It matches the author peek the desktop app got in this release; nothing on desktop changes.
Before
The author in the web Mods list, and under a mod's name in the mod panel, was plain text.
After
It is a real button. It opens a side panel with the author's name, how many projects they have, their total downloads, Open on CurseForge or Modrinth, and every project: icon, name, short description, type, downloads and when it last changed, with Installed on the ones this server has. An installed project opens its mod panel; any other opens its page in the same panel, with Back to <author>.
Good to know
Below 1150 px the panel lies over the list instead of pushing the page, the same as the existing mod panel. The Author column drops out once a panel is open and the list gets under 1050 px; the mod panel's by <author> button is the way in then, and at phone width that panel now fits the window instead of being cut off on the left. Browse results and the new server pack picker keep the author as plain text: the row itself opens the project page, and its byline already shows everything that author made.
  • crates/hopperd/ui/src/AuthorPeek.svelte (new)
  • crates/hopperd/ui/src/Mods.svelte
  • crates/hopperd/ui/src/ModPeek.svelte
  • crates/hopperd/ui/src/BrowsePack.svelte
seen on screen
  • A scratch Hopper Server with one throwaway server, seen in a real browser at 1440, 1200, 900 and 375 wide, dark and light: the author button and its ring, the side panel beside the list and over it, an installed project opening its mod panel, another project's page in the panel and Back, on a CurseForge mod and on a Modrinth one.
  • Measured on the running page: Tab reaches the author button and its ring is 2 px in the accent colour; the author text reads at 5.89 to 1 on dark and 5.74 to 1 on light, and the panel title at 14.05 and 16.12. The page keeps a 12 px gap beside the panel, no row clips or wraps at any width, and the panel fits a 375 px phone screen (351 px wide).
  • Tests passed: every test program, including 427 checks in Hopper Server with 4 ignored, and the web page built cleanly.
partly checked
  • Not checked: a CurseForge-only server without an API key (the panel shows its own message for that; not seen); GitHub and link mods (they have no author button, by design); desktop (unchanged); zoom.
  • Not measured: pixel gaps inside rows (icon against text), hover colours, zoom other than 100%, themes other than Hopper Dark and Light, and the text fade in the pack description in the light theme (it looks pale; that is the existing pack page, not touched).
  • No before pictures: the old build was not kept. Before, the author was plain text in the Author column.

Hopper 0.83.2 · Hopper Server 0.78.3

18 Nov 2026

Relay setup: Hopper reads the VPS's host key before it sends your password or key

Apps
Hopper Server's relay setup page, and the Hopper desktop dialog that drives the same setup (the card below). Both had to change, because the sign-in now goes out only once the key is confirmed.
Before
"Connect" signed in at once: the password went out, or your key was offered, to whatever answered at that address, and the VPS's host key was shown afterwards. Someone sitting between you and the VPS at first setup could have taken the password.
After
"Connect" now only reads the host key and sends nothing. The step shows the key and asks you to compare it with the one your VPS provider shows (on their page or in the console). "It matches: sign in" sends the sign-in, locked to that key. "How do I check this?" opens the command that prints the key on the VPS. When the address answers with a key other than the one saved before (after a reinstall, say), the step shows the new key above the saved one and keeps the orange button off until you tick "I reinstalled the VPS: trust this new host key".
Good to know
Once the key is trusted, the rest of the setup works as before: install, repair and the checks after it reconnect only with the saved key. An older Hopper desktop cannot set up a relay on this version until it is updated (the server asks for the checked key first), and a new desktop against an older server stops with the server's own message. Neither one sends the password unchecked.
  • crates/hopperd/src/vps/setup/ssh.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/vps/setup/mod.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/vps/setup/host_key_tests.rs
  • crates/hopperd/ui/src/VpsRelaySetup.svelte
  • src/gpui/relay_setup.rs
  • design/vps-relay.md
seen on screen
  • A scratch Hopper Server and a stand-in SSH server, driven by a real browser: the step that shows the key, the same in the light theme, the "How do I check this?" command, the changed key, and the step on a phone-width screen. The stand-in server's log showed no password and no key offered while the step was open, one password exactly after "It matches", and key sign-ins only after "Trust the new key".
  • Measured on screen: the step fits the window at 560 px in a 1100 px window, and 343 px on a 375 px phone screen, with no sideways scroll. The whole key is visible, wrapped onto two lines on the phone; its text reads at 15.3 to 1 on dark and 16.5 to 1 on light.
  • Tests passed: 27 for the setup, 6 of them new. A wrong or empty key pin sends no password and offers no key; an empty, wrong or "any" key is refused with nothing sent; the right key signs in once; a saved key is reused and a different one is refused; the route refuses anyone who is not signed in; the key is read strictly and the pin is never empty.
partly checked
  • Not checked: a real VPS and a real provider page, and the light theme's orange button (white on orange, 3.3 to 1). That button is the app's shared style, not part of this change, and it is the one thing here that reads faintly.
  • No "before" picture: the old build was not to hand, and the old flow had no such step. It signed in at once, then showed the key.

Relay setup on desktop: the same check, seen running in the app

Apps
Hopper desktop. The server half is the card above; the dialog here only had to follow it, so the two cannot drift apart.
Before
The desktop dialog signed in at once as well, so the password or key went out before you had seen the key. The new step existed but had not been run in the app.
After
The desktop dialog has the same steps as the server's page, word for word: the key and "Copy", "How do I check this?", the changed-key warning with its tick, and Back to the form with the address, user, mode and password still filled in. Seen in the app in dark and light, in a wide and in a small window, with real clicks.
Good to know
One small look difference: on desktop the first line of the changed-key warning sits on its own line and is bold, where the page has it bold inside the sentence. Same words, same tick. On the changed key the orange button stays off until the tick, and clicking the tick again turns it off again.

  • src/gpui/app.rs
seen on screen
  • A test build in the app, with made-up data and no server behind it: the key first time, the changed key, the tick on and off, "How do I check this?" open, Back, and signed in, in dark and light at a wide and a small window size. The dialog goes back to the form with everything you typed still filled in.

  • Not checked: the step against a real Hopper Server and a real VPS (the made-up data has no server behind it, so Connect and the confirm only reach the error note, which shows and leaves the dialog usable); keyboard focus rings, a screen reader, and zoom other than 100%.

Relay: floods, crowded networks and full relays no longer get in the way

Apps
Hopper Server only, in the part that carries players through a relay. Nothing in Hopper desktop changes.
Before
Connections that never finish could pile up as high as they liked, so a flood could tie up a relay for everyone. Someone using a run of addresses in the same network slipped past the per-player limit. A relay with no room left answered "offline", so a busy server looked like a dead one. A server set to Relay mode could still be reached directly, without Velocity. And a home that had just had failed attempts kept them in the way after a good connection.
After
An address can hold 4 unfinished connections, and all addresses together 64, so a flood cannot fill the queue. Players from one network (an IPv4 /24 or an IPv6 /64) count together: 8 of them at a time. Logins keep their room: at most 192 new direct connections in ten seconds, so part of the relay stays free for players who are already in and for Hopper's own link. A relay with no room says the server is full instead of offline. A server set to Relay mode accepts only players who come through Velocity at both ends, and refuses a direct join. A link that works clears the failed attempts from that address, so a PC that reconnects gets straight in.
Good to know
Nothing to set up, and no setting to change. These are guards that only matter while something is flooding the relay. A big school, office or mobile network, where many players share one IPv4 /24, counts as one network for the 8-player limit.
  • crates/hopperd/src/vps/relay_end.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/vps/home.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/vps/tests.rs
partly checked
  • No screenshots: nothing looks different, and neither app was run on screen for this. The change is in how the relay takes connections.
  • Tests passed: each address and the total number of unfinished connections are capped and freed again when a player leaves; players behind one /24 or /64 count together, and addresses written as IPv4 count too; the room kept for logins comes back when a player leaves or the wait runs out; a full relay answers full, not offline, and so does a full login room; players coming through Velocity can use the kept room; the list of recent failed attempts cannot grow past its cap; a home reconnects with a half-open connection and clears its failures. Ten runs of the relay tests in a row passed, 77 each; the full Hopper Server suite passed too.
  • Not checked: a real Minecraft server with real players through a real relay, Velocity itself, a live deployment, and mixed versions of the two apps.

Behind the scenes: relay tests wait until the routes are in place

Apps
Hopper Server tests only. Nothing looks different.
Before
One test sent a join before the relay had the new route, and failed now and then when the timing was off.
After
The test waits until the relay reports the routes, then checks once. A join with no route, an unknown address, and a route whose port was removed are still refused, as they should be.
  • crates/hopperd/src/vps/relay_end.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/vps/tests.rs
partly checked
  • No screenshots: no player-visible change.
  • Tests passed: 15 runs of the relay tests in a row, 69 each, and the full Hopper Server suite (one check that needs a built web UI in that folder failed; this change does not touch it).

Friends groups: a mod's missing required mod is asked about

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server has no friends groups.
Before
You add a mod that needs another mod (Shapewright needs owo-lib) to a group. Every PC says "In step", nobody is told, and the pack can't start.
After
When you add or update a mod in a group, a dialog offers to add the mods it needs too ("Add owo (owo-lib) to Testers too?"). Any PC whose copy of the pack still lacks a required mod shows "Missing a required mod" in the group strip, with an Add it button, instead of "In step". Good to know
You see another member's missing mod only after their pack has synced to your PC. Mods that need a different version, or a mod site you turned off, stay on the Mods tab banner. Adding a mod through a script (MCP) does not ask.

Before: "Turned off <mod> for <group>" stayed on screen for minutes. After: it goes away on its own after about 5 seconds, and waits while you hover it.

Hopper Server 0.78.0

17 Nov 2026

Several servers behind one relay

Apps
Hopper Server web page only. Hopper desktop doesn't show relays yet, and the new Relays page says so.
Before
One relay could carry only one server. If you put a second server behind it, every player still landed on the first.
After
One relay can carry several servers. Each server has its own address, and players type that address to reach it. A new Relays page in Settings lists the relay and the servers behind it, each with the address players join at, who checks accounts, how many players are on, and a Take off button. A server's relay card now says when it has no address of its own, or when its address isn't on the relay yet, with a Repair button. An address the relay doesn't know gets a plain refusal: "No server here goes by this address. Check the address and try again."
Good to know
Voice goes through the relay for the first server with a voice mod only; the other servers' cards say so. Changing the relay's Velocity setup restarts Velocity for a few seconds, which drops players on the other servers while it comes back. With only one server left on a relay, any address reaches it, so joining by IP keeps working.

Hopper 0.81.0 · Hopper Server 0.76.0

17 Nov 2026

Config sliders follow the pointer; the mouse wheel turns number fields

Apps
Both. Hopper desktop (the Configs tab and the other number fields) and the Hopper Server web config editor (and every number field there).
Before
On desktop, dragging a config slider did not keep up: the handle and the number stayed on the old value while you dragged, and a redraw could snap the handle back. Decimal sliders moved in uneven steps, and the mouse wheel did nothing on numbers. On the web, the wheel did nothing on config numbers and decimal sliders always stepped by a fixed 0.1 or 0.01.
After
On desktop the handle and the number follow your pointer, the value stays where you let go, and the ends hold. Decimal sliders step in tidy amounts. Scroll the wheel over a number to change it (hold Shift for ten times as much); it stops at the minimum and maximum, and the page does not scroll. The - and + buttons use the same steps. The web editor now follows the same step rules, and the wheel works on config numbers and every other number field.

Hopper 0.79.0 · Hopper Server 0.74.0

16 Nov 2026

Friend groups: a mod removed while a friend plays stays removed

Apps
Hopper desktop (and the shared code, so Hopper Server's mod lists follow the same rule). Hopper Server takes no part in friend groups.
Before
You removed a mod from a group pack while a friend was in game. If their game held the jar, it waited in their mods folder, and any older copy of their mod list read back meanwhile (CurseForge's own file, or a stale save as the game closed) listed it again. Their next update then sent it back to everyone as their own newest change.
After
A jar waiting for the game to let go counts as gone, so nothing can list it again. The play time saved when a game ends no longer writes the whole, possibly older, in-memory instance over the file.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/instances.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/groupsync.rs
  • src/gpui/store.rs
partly checked
  • Three new tests fail with the fix switched off and pass with it, one of them end to end: a member who plays while a mod is removed, then closes the game (Windows only, it holds the jar the way Java does). The shared library's suite passed (1,538 tests, none failed) and the desktop app compiles.
  • No screenshots: nothing looks different, this is sync logic only.
  • Not checked: the friend's own PC. The sharer's data shows the mod was listed again with a new date, but not which path on that PC did it. If a game lets the jar move while it plays (NeoForge can), the jar was never queued and this fix does not cover that case.
  • Not done: Undo of a removal whose jar is still waiting does not unqueue it yet, so the jar still leaves at game end.

Discord is a switch you can see, and Hopper Server leaves SFTP off until you turn it on

Apps
Hopper desktop (setup and two captions) and Hopper Server (its SFTP and update check).
Before
Showing what you play on Discord was on with no word about it in setup. Every new Hopper Server listened for SFTP on port 8022. The panel asked GitHub for new versions every 30 minutes with no way to stop it. The README said playing without Mojang's launcher needs an extra setting.
After
Setup has a "Show what I'm playing on Discord" switch with a line saying what friends see; it saves when you click it, and nothing changes for people who already set it. A new Hopper Server has SFTP off, and Settings › Access & devices has an SFTP card with a switch that works at once and closes open connections when turned off (a server that already names a port keeps it). Settings › Updates has "Look for new versions" (on by default); off means no checks every half hour, and "Check now" still works. Playing without Mojang's launcher works out of the box in releases.

New instances get memory that fits the pack and this PC

Apps
Hopper desktop and Hopper Server (its New server window uses the same rule).
Before
Every new instance got a flat 8 GB, and a CurseForge pack took the author's number as it was, even 12 GB on an 8 GB PC.
After
A new instance takes the pack's recommended memory, or an estimate from its mod count (4 GB for a small pack up to 12 GB for a big one, one step lower on Fabric and Quilt), then fits it to this PC so Windows keeps at least 4 GB or 30% free. Settings shows "Auto (44 GB)" with a line saying why, and it is picked again at every launch, so it follows mods added later and pack updates. The first Play says once when the PC could not give what the pack asks for. Memory you set by hand always wins, and instances made before this change keep their 8 GB because they ran fine.

Friends: changes show up at once, with ten times fewer calls to the sync service

Apps
Hopper desktop and the sync service. Hopper Server has no Friends panel.
Before
The desktop read the friend list every 3 seconds: about 1,200 calls an hour for every player, even with nothing changed. The panel said nothing about the connection unless it was down.
After
The sync service now says in the request it already holds open when a friend list changes, and the desktop reads the list only then. One idle player went from 1,194 friend-list reads an hour to 12. A safety read every 5 minutes, a read when the window comes back, and a retry 20 seconds after a failed one cover the rest. A quiet line at the bottom of every Friends page says Connected, Connecting, Reconnecting or Offline. Against an older sync service Hopper falls back to reading every 30 seconds.

Deleting something now waits a week in Recently deleted

Apps
Hopper desktop and Hopper Server (the server's own trash now uses the same code and the same words).
Before
Deleting an instance, a world or a backup sent it to the Recycle Bin (copied to C: when it was on another drive), or deleted it for good if "Skip Recycle Bin" was on. Nothing in Hopper could bring it back.
After
A delete moves the folder into a hidden one on the same drive, which is a rename, not a copy. It waits 7 days (30 on Hopper Server) and is removed after that. Settings › Library & folders lists what is there with Restore, a bin button and "Empty now". Every delete window has a "Delete for good now (can't be undone)" tick box that changes the button to "Delete for good". Screenshots, logs, loose files and server backups go the same way. An instance comes back with its group and settings, and a world whose instance is still deleted is refused with a hint to restore that first.

  • Measured: a strip is 67 px tall open and closed (it used to shrink when opened), every colour reads at least 5 to 1 in both themes, and at 960 pt with a side panel open nothing is clipped. On the web page a strip is 36 px and at 560 px wide nothing passes the strip's edge.
  • The screenshots found and fixed eight things: a shrinking strip, a dark red title at 3.69 to 1, buttons that did not line up, a duplicate button, a subject squeezed to three letters, a banner title under the close button, and two clipping bugs on the web page.
partly checked
  • Not checked on screen: five or more notices on the web page (that server reaches three), the restart notice (it needs a running server), and the one-notice state in a browser.
  • Found and not fixed: at 960 pt the crash card's "Then the game crashed in…" line runs past the window edge, and a config side panel can keep another instance's config after switching instance.
  • Identifying, where-it-came-from and CurseForge key notices stay on the Mods tab only, because they describe that tab's own scan.

Server panels: trust a self-made certificate once, then only that one

Apps
Hopper desktop. Hopper Server's own pairing already pins its certificate.
Before
A Crafty, MCSS or Pterodactyl panel with a self-made certificate accepted any certificate at all, and Hopper then sent the panel's API key to whoever answered.
After
The first Save and test shows the certificate's fingerprint and asks "Trust this panel's certificate?", and nothing is sent until you answer. After that Hopper connects only while the panel has that certificate. If it changes, Hopper stops before sending the key and shows the old and new fingerprint; "Trust the new certificate" works only after ticking "I changed the panel's certificate myself". A panel saved before this pins the certificate it has on the next connect and says so once, and a panel with a real certificate keeps the normal check.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/tls.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/panel.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/settings.rs
  • src/gpui/settings_page.rs
  • src/gpui/store.rs
seen on screen
  • Desktop on a test PC against a fake panel with two self-made certificates: Save and test showed the fingerprint and the fake panel logged no request; after Trust the first key-bearing request arrived. Swapping the certificate stopped Hopper before any request, the button stayed disabled until ticked, and trusting the new one connected.
  • A panel saved before this pinned itself on the next start and said so once, and a certificate swapped back was caught by the background check before any Test.
  • Measured: the card has the same 30 px inset on both sides in all four states, its text reads 14.92 to 1 dark and 15.34 to 1 light, and the whole fingerprint fits on one line.
partly checked
  • Not checked on screen: MCSS and Pterodactyl (same code, different panel), a real certificate-signed panel, and the narrowest window, where the fingerprint line would wrap.
  • The trust card in the dark theme after its colour change was re-shot in light only; the measured error card uses the same code.
  • Also fixed on the way: the panel address used to blank itself when settings changed while the window was open.

Apps

Before

A changed certificate stops before the password, shows old and new, and "Trust the new certificate" works only after ticking "I changed the server's certificate myself". A site saved before this pins its certificate on the next connect and says so once, and a normal certificate is checked as before.

Hopper lets go of description pictures and GIFs

Apps
Hopper desktop. Hopper Server's pages leave images to the browser.
Before
Every picture and GIF frame from a description page stayed in memory, on the processor and on the graphics card, until Hopper quit. Memory climbed with each page and never came back down.
After
Description pictures share the picture cache's 96 MB budget and are freed when it trims. GIFs are limited by size as well as by count, and one no page has drawn for 20 seconds is freed; a page that shows it again decodes it again. After a long browsing session with a minute idle, private memory went from 1,274 MB to 639 MB.
  • src/gpui/widgets.rs
  • src/gpui/thumbs.rs
  • src/gpui/anim.rs
  • src/gpui/store.rs
seen on screen
  • Two release builds side by side on a test PC with the same script on each: 30 project pages (scrolling each description), then 30 pages of GIF-heavy searches, then a minute idle. Private memory at the end: 1,274 MB before, 639 MB after, and the old build was still climbing.
  • Description pictures look and lay out the same: nothing differs by more than 16 of 255, on about a tenth of a percent of pixels. GIFs still play, and one let go while another page was open played again.
  • Tests passed: 5 new for the rules about when a GIF may be freed, and the whole desktop suite.
partly checked
  • Not checked: scroll smoothness (the test hooks cannot time frames), the moment a GIF shows its first frame again, and the light theme, which looks the same before and after.
  • Left as is: 20 graphics textures are still held while idle, from the picture cache's own copies, within its 96 MB budget, and "clear image cache" in Settings still misses their graphics copies.

Hopper sleeps when nothing is happening

Apps
Hopper desktop. Hopper Server has no such timers.
Before
With nothing open, Hopper woke about 33 times a second for its test hooks, every 3 seconds for the Downloads folder, every 5 seconds for a paired panel, every 3 seconds for the History number, and read Browse's Minecraft version list at startup.
After
Each timer runs only while its feature is in use, and starts the moment it is switched on. The History number updates when something changes instead of on a timer, and the version list loads the first time Browse opens. Nothing on screen changed.

Hopper 0.77.2 · Hopper Server 0.72.2

16 Nov 2026

Undo works for mods only the CurseForge website hands out

Apps
Hopper desktop and Hopper Server (the same fix in the shared code; the server panel shows a message).
Before
Updating or removing a mod whose author turned off downloads by other apps (Structory: Towers) deleted its file from History. Undo then failed with "X can only be downloaded from the CurseForge website".
After
History now keeps the real file for such mods, so Undo just works. Older History entries that only hold a note: Undo opens Hopper's download window for the file and adds it when it arrives. On Hopper Server the message says which file to download and from which page, then add it to the server's mods. Each file set aside now asks the site once; offline, the file is kept (safe).

Tasks wait their turn; "Roll back" in the load-problem notice

Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server has no "another task" message on mod actions and no load-problem notice.
Before
Starting an Undo, install or update while another task changed the same instance was refused: "Another task is changing this instance". A mod file that an update broke only offered "Turn off".
After
The new task waits in line for that instance. The task list shows it with an hourglass and "Waiting for '<task>' to finish", it can be cancelled while it waits, and it re-reads the instance when its turn comes. "Close the game first" and Delete instance still refuse. In the notice, an outlined "Roll back" sits next to "Turn off" when History has the update that brought the current file. Its tooltip reads "Back to 15.10.4, from before the update on 16 Nov". It runs History's Undo, then the notice checks again.

Updates never swap a 1.21 mod for a file made for Minecraft 26.x

Apps
Hopper desktop and Hopper Server (updates, installs and dependencies share the same rule).
Before
"Update all" on a 1.21.1 NeoForge instance replaced Structory: Towers 1.0.15 with a file built for Minecraft 26.2, which NeoForge rejects. The author had tagged that one file for both 1.21 and 26.x, and Hopper trusted the tags.
After
A mod file that lists any year-named version (26.1, 26.2...) no longer counts as made for a 1.x instance. Updates, installs from Browse, dependencies and the Versions list's "fits this instance" pick the newest file that really fits. On a 26.x instance the same file still fits. Resource packs and shaders tagged for both still fit. An instance already on the wrong file is not offered a downgrade: pick the old version in Versions by hand.

Apps
Hopper desktop and the Hopper Server panel it talks to. The server's web page has no sync push, so it has nothing to change.

After
Config files are read in one request. No compares run during a sync, and one runs at the end. If the panel asks to slow down, the server list and the Sync tab stay on screen and say "Hopper Server asked to slow down. Trying again in N s." and try again by themselves. Each ticked mod shows its own state: Waiting, Uploading N%, Added or Updated, or Failed with the reason in a tooltip and a Try again button. The footer says "Pushing 3 of 7…" with a small bar. In narrow windows the row shows only the % or an icon.

Hopper 0.77.1 · Hopper Server 0.72.1

16 Nov 2026

Check for updates now sees a GitHub release made minutes ago

Apps
Hopper desktop and Hopper Server (the same fix on both sides).
Before
Pressing "Check for updates" within 10 minutes of the last look reused Hopper's saved list of GitHub releases without asking GitHub, so a mod release made minutes ago showed "Up to date". When GitHub was limiting checks, the Mods tab said nothing, or showed a red error when nothing was saved.
After
A check you start always asks GitHub right away: the Mods tab check button, "Check for updates" in the palette, Look up in "Add from GitHub or a link", a mod's Versions list, and on Hopper Server the check button on a server's Mods page and its Versions list. When nothing changed, GitHub's answer is free and does not count toward its limit. Start-up and background checks still reuse an answer from the last few minutes. When GitHub is limiting checks, the Mods tab says so and when GitHub mods are checked again ("GitHub is limiting checks for now. Mods from GitHub are checked again at 14:05.") instead of "Up to date". The Hopper Server page says the same instead of "Every mod is up to date".

The sides are saved, so they show at once after a restart.

  • Hopper desktop on a test PC, a throwaway NeoForge 1.21.1 instance with Inventory Profiles Next, Just Enough Items and a made-up mod. Mods tab dark at 1440 wide, and light at the smallest window (960 by 600) with and without the mod page open. The chip's tooltip and the side card showed "from Modrinth".   • The pack update window and the Friends lists show no side, so they did not change.

Server sync: client-only pack mods stay off the server list again

Apps
Hopper desktop (the shared code in hopper-core). Hopper Server has no sync of its own to change.
Before
After the author of a modpack replaced its server pack on CurseForge, Hopper could no longer read the server pack it had saved the file number of. The server sync page then went only by CurseForge's Client tags, and a client-only mod with no tag (Inventory Profiles Next in TNP Limitless 8) showed up as a mod to put on the server. Making a server from the pack could fail the same way.
After
If the saved server pack can't be read, Hopper asks the pack for its current server pack and uses that (and remembers it, so later checks don't ask again). Making a server from a pack follows a replaced server pack the same way. Without a readable server pack, mods with no CurseForge side tag are checked on Modrinth too, and client-only there means the mod stays off the server ("Client-only on Modrinth"). A CurseForge tag still wins, and so does "Sync anyway". Nothing needs re-importing: the next sync check finds the new server pack.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/serverpack.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/hosting/server_pack.rs
partly checked
  • No screenshots: not seen on screen. It needs a paired test Hopper Server and a CurseForge pack whose server pack was replaced.
  • Tests passed: all hopper-core tests, 1461, including 2 new ones: untagged pack mods go by Modrinth without a server pack (it fails on the old code), and a replaced server pack is followed.
  • Cause found on a real pack: the author replaced the 1.80.0 server pack on 15 November, so the file Hopper recorded no longer exists. It was not a Hopper change.

Hopper 0.77.0

15 Nov 2026

Friends: see which changes are still waiting to be sent, and why a send failed

Apps
Hopper desktop. Hopper Server has no Friends or group sync.
Before
A change made while offline, or one the sync service refused, vanished silently. Nothing said it had not reached the group, and "storage full" only said something went wrong. Members never saw the names of the sharer's hand-added jars. The Follow dialog said "your copy updates when X changes theirs" even for group packs, where changes go both ways.
After
Waiting changes show as a number on the Friends icon and one line in the Friends panel: "waiting to be sent" (they go out by themselves when Hopper can connect again), "Sending your changes to X…", or "Hopper tries again in a minute". The number clears once the send goes through. A refused send shows one notice with a reason that says what to do. Members see hand-added jars by name ("X isn't on any mod site. Ask Ada to send it."). The Follow dialog says changes go both ways for a group pack and one way for a friend's direct share.
  • src/gpui/store/friends.rs
  • src/gpui/store.rs
  • src/gpui/app.rs
  • src/gpui/friends.rs
  • src/gpui/peers.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/mailbox.rs
seen on screen
  • Two test copies of Hopper on a test PC (throwaway data, loopback only, no real friends, groups or packs). Offline: cut one copy's connection and turned a mod off; the number and line showed. Reconnected through a slow proxy: "Sending…", then sent, and the number cleared. The proxy then answered "storage full": the notice showed once and the number stayed at 1. The retry came every 60 s (22:17:49, 22:18:49, 22:19:49). Opened both Follow dialogs from the second copy.
  • Measured: the Friends number is 28 by 28 px, the same as the History number, and its digit is centred (0 px off) in dark and light. Digit contrast 6.76 to 1 dark, 5.18 to 1 light; the panel line 14.39 and 14.34 to 1. At the smallest window (960 pt) with the panel open the number is whole and the line wraps onto two lines inside its bar.
  • Tests passed: sync service tests 16 (including the new "full says what makes room"), the storage-full scenario, and 5 Friends store tests (including the sharer's hand-added names and the waiting-line wording).
partly checked
  • Not checked: the failure notice and the Follow dialogs on the light theme, zoom levels other than 100%, a really full sync service (the proxy sent its exact answer), and the "friends queue full" wording on screen (a test covers it).
  • Only the Friends icon has a before picture; the rest are new, or the old build was not run.
  • Left as is: the first look at each shared pack after Hopper starts counts as already sent, so a change made while Hopper was closed, or one that failed just before quitting, is not counted after a restart. Going back to a mod list that was already sent shows no number, because nothing new needs to go out. Unreachable sends stay out of the notice on purpose: the "Can't connect" bar already covers them.
  • Hand-added jars that live only in the sharer's mods folder were not part of the share when this was built; the next card in this release names them.

Friends: ask before getting a group pack, and a Try again button after Hopper gives up

Apps
Hopper desktop. Hopper Server has no Friends or groups.
Before
With "Keep my packs the same as the group" on, Hopper downloaded every pack shared to the group by itself. An update that gave up after 10 tries only sat in the jobs list. A "couldn't save" answer from the sync service read as "can't reach". Mods you locked were kept during a group merge without a word. Jars that only sat in the sharer's mods folder were left out of the share.
After
One notice per shared pack: "Mia shared Mia's Skyblock. Get it?" with Not now (remembered for that pack) and Get (the usual Follow flow). An update that gave up shows a warning bar at the top of the Friends panel with an outlined Try again button. "Couldn't save" now reads "Friends couldn't save your change just now. Hopper sends it again by itself." and Hopper keeps trying. A locked mod that stayed as it is is named once per merge, with the reason. Hand-added jars are named in the share.

Group sync: your undo is not lost, locked mods stay, failing updates stop retrying

Apps
Hopper desktop logic in the shared code (hopper-core). No screen changed in this card. Hopper Server has no Friends sync.
Before
A member's undo of the sharer's change was lost if the member sent another change before the sharer came back, and the two copies stayed different for good, with no message. A group change could update, remove or put back the file of a mod you had locked. A group update that always failed was retried every 15 seconds forever.
After
The sharer takes the undo too. A locked mod keeps its file (turning it on or off still syncs), and the merge says what was kept and why ("Sodium stays as it is: you locked its version. Mia changed its version."). After 10 quick tries Hopper stops retrying that code by itself and says so once; internet, mod site and held-file failures keep trying every 5 minutes, and a newer code is tried at once.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/groupsync.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/recipe.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/tests/sync_scenarios.rs
partly checked
  • No screenshots: logic only, and the app was not run for this card. The wording and the Try again button for these cases are on the card above.
  • Tests passed: the offline-scenario suite went from 22 passed and 5 failed to 23 passed and 4 failed; the undo case now passes and the other four known gaps are untouched. Seven new unit tests cover the undo, the locked mod and the retry cap. All hopper-core tests: 1457 passed.
  • Not checked: a real locked mod in a real group merge (only a test instance on disk).
  • Left as is: a change to a locked mod is taken in and not retried, so unlocking later does not bring it back; the next change to that mod does. Only a full lock counts, not "skip one update" of a mod.

Friends sync: a change sent just before the sync service crashed is no longer lost

Apps
The sync service only (relay/hopper_mailbox.py). No desktop or Hopper Server code changed.
Before
The sync service answered a send first and saved it about 1.5 s later. A crash in between lost the change, Hopper had already counted it as sent, and it was never sent again.
After
Every change is saved to disk (temp file, flush, atomic rename) before the service answers. If the save fails it answers "couldn't save", Hopper keeps the change and sends it again. Friend and group changes (join, invite, unfriend, create, edit, leave and the rest) are saved first too. A waiting long-poll only hands out changes that are already on disk, so a friend can never hear of a change that a crash would take back.
  • relay/hopper_mailbox.py
  • relay/test_hopper_mailbox.py
  • relay/README.md
  • design/friends-sync.md
partly checked
  • No screenshots: nothing looks different. Tested on a local test copy of the service first. Deployed to the live sync service on 15 November with no save errors in its log.
  • Tests passed: 92 service tests (86 before; 3 old ones rewritten, 6 new), including a service killed hard right after it answered a send and restarted with the change still there. The offline-scenario suite went from 22 passed to 23: the crash case now passes.
  • Measured on this PC: one send costs a median of 9 ms with a 1.1 MB state (was 4 ms), 19 ms at 5.4 MB, and 65 ms at 24.8 MB (the size limit; slowest 82 ms).
  • Not checked: a real power cut, many sends at once on the live server, and the real disk speed there (it may be slower than this PC).
  • The save file format is unchanged: no migration, and an old service can read a new file and the other way round.

Behind the scenes: offline group sync tested end to end

Apps
Hopper desktop and the sync service. Tests only in this card, no fixes. Hopper Server has no Friends sync.

After

Three of those gaps are fixed by other cards in this release (undo lost, change lost in a crash, "storage full" wording). Two remain: the change made last should win whoever comes back first, and a pack pushed out of a full queue is lost silently. Two test copies of Hopper on a test PC confirmed the main cases on screen.

seen on screen

partly checked
  • Not checked on screen: three members at once, a member removed, a group deleted or a pack unshared while someone is offline, the queue-full wording, a crash in the middle of an apply, a loader change, and Hopper Server.
gap found
  • Found and not fixed (not sync): the "Client only" chip is cut off on a mod row that has no version: 80 px wide against 112 px on the Sodium row, 32 px cut.

Server History opens about ten times faster; truer pack update wording

Apps
Hopper Server (History speed, pack update toast) and desktop (pack update window line, toast for a server's pack update, Backups sizes). One History route serves both.
Before
History on a server with 386 entries took about 2.5 seconds to open. The pack update window said "Nothing of yours in the pack's folders" even when you had dropped a jar into the mods folder. A server's pack update toast always said "updated to".
After
History opens in about 0.44 seconds the first time and about 0.23 seconds after that. A jar you put in the mods folder by hand counts as "N mods you added", and the line now says "Nothing of yours in the mods folder or the pack's folders". A server's pack toast says "reinstalled at" (same version), "rolled back to" (older) or "updated to", like an instance's.

Undo brings back a Modrinth pack mod's own file; updating a turned-off mod keeps it off

Apps
Both. The fix is in the shared code that desktop and Hopper Server use for updates and group sync.
Before
In a group on a Modrinth pack, when a member undid an update or removal of a pack mod, the others only got a note and kept the update (or lost the mod outright). History Undo on a Modrinth file always fetched the version's main file, so it failed when the pack used a different file. Updating a mod you had turned off could bring the new file in turned on, if you turned it off while the game was running or if the file on disk was already off.
After
The pack's exact old file comes back from the pack's own download, checked against the pack, turned on and counted as the pack's again; the change goes to History. Undo fetches the file with the right checksum. An update of a turned-off mod follows what you see in the Mods tab (a waiting switch first, then the file on disk), so it stays off.

  • crates/hopper-core/src/backups.rs
partly checked
  • No screenshots: nothing looks different, and neither app was run for this.
  • Five new tests cover the group put-back (pack copy updated, fresh copy, removed), the two turned-off cases, and picking the Modrinth file by checksum; the fixes failed them before. Hopper core: 1434 passed.
  • Not checked: a real Modrinth download in either place, and a full multi-PC group run on a Modrinth pack.

Hopper 0.74.1

15 Nov 2026

Group packs: Undo in History reaches the group and stays undone

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Group sync is desktop only; Hopper Server has no group copies.
Before
Undo of an update in a group pack put the old file back on your PC only. The group kept the update, and the mod's row could read "Removed by Mia" while the mod was there. Two causes: a quick Undo looked like no change to the others, and undoing a change to a pack's own mod was never sent.
After
Undo is now sent like any other change. Each code marks the mods it changed itself, and the others take those changes even when the code looks the same as the last one. A member who gets "as the pack has it" for a pack mod puts the pack's own file back (CurseForge packs). The row reads "Updated by Mia". Modrinth packs still only note that putting back a pack mod isn't done by sync.

Polytone 6.8.6 to 6.10.1: I updated then Undid (jar back to 6.8.6, and the code sent carried it); Mia updated and her code said "as the pack has it" (6.8.6 put back here); Mia updated, I Undid, and a late echo of her update was ignored. The jar stayed 6.8.6 for 160 s.
  • Measured: the label has the same size and padding apart from the text (1 to 2 px of anti-aliasing), and the text reads at 7.5 to 1 in both versions.

  • Left as it is: History words an Undo as "You switched Polytone 26.2-6.10.1", not "put back 6.8.6".

Hopper 0.74.0 · Hopper Server 0.69.0

15 Nov 2026

Pack updates run in the background and end with a toast

Apps
Both. Hopper desktop has everything below. Hopper Server's web page closes its dialog on Update, shows a chip on the server page and the same result wording.
Before
The pack update window stayed open with its own progress until the update ended. The CurseForge download window opened white, in front of Hopper, and took focus. Repair could not fetch mods that only the CurseForge website hands out, and a paired server was updated before those files arrived, so it missed them.
After
Update closes the window at once. Progress shows on the instance row (a line and a bar), in the header and in Tasks. When it ends a toast says how it went: updated, reinstalled or rolled back, plus "N files came from the CurseForge website", or a warning with Retry when some did not download, or an error. The download window opens behind Hopper in Hopper's colours and never takes focus. If CurseForge asks for its bot check, a toast says the update is waiting for you, with Show window. Repair fetches website-only mods through the same window, and the server update waits for those files first.

Groups: a member's pack update reaches everyone

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server has no group copies.
Before
When a member updated a group's pack (for example 8.5 to 8.6) nothing about the version was sent. Everyone else stayed on 8.5, and a mod that member had added themselves that 8.6 now includes was read as "removed" and deleted for everyone.
After
The pack version is part of what a group keeps. When a member moves it forward or back, the others run the full pack update themselves, keeping their own edits, and the History entry carries that member's name. A notice says who moved it. If your game is running, your copy updates when you close it. If the update needs a choice (a file you and the pack both changed) the notice says so and has a button that opens the update window. If it fails, the notice says why and tries again in 10 minutes, or on Try again.

Pack updates keep mods you updated, mods you turned off, and the pack's own files

Apps
Hopper desktop and group follows (both use the same update plan). Hopper Server's server pack update already kept the instance's versions, so it was left alone.
Before
A normal "Keep my changes" pack update put every pack mod you had updated back to the pack's older file, with no question. It also removed a mod file the pack ships itself (a mod no longer on CurseForge) whenever your copy was already the same file.
After
When no older pack version is known, a mod file newer than the pack's counts as yours. It shows under "Mods you updated" with Keep mine picked, and the pack's file is still picked where the pack's is newer. Mods you turned off arrive as the pack's new file and stay off. A file the new version ships itself is never listed or dropped as removed. A clean update still resets everything to the pack.

Modrinth pack updates keep mods whose file gets a new name

Apps
Both. The shared update plan is used by the desktop update window, by group follows and by Hopper Server.
Before
Updating a Modrinth pack (compared with the installed version, which every update without hand-picked choices does) listed every mod whose file name changed as "Mods you removed" and left it out. Remarkably Optimized 1.15.66 to 1.15.70 went from 27 mods to 20.
After
Only mods with no copy left in the folder (on or off) count as removed. The same update ends with the pack's 28 mods, minus the one the player removed, plus the player's own mod; a mod you turned off arrives turned off under its new name.

Relay through a VPS: voice chat goes through the relay too

Apps
Hopper Server only. The desktop's relay card has no voice row yet.
Before
With a server on the VPS relay, Simple Voice Chat and Plasmo Voice still told players to connect to the home address, so voice did not work for relayed players unless a port was open at home.
After
The relay carries voice (UDP) too: players reach the VPS on UDP 24454 and the home panel dials out, sealed with keys from the tunnel. "Use the relay" points the voice mod at the relay, and the change is in History and undone by "Play at home" or "Stop using the relay". The relay card gets a voice row. If UDP can't get through, voice falls back to the tunnel and the card says "Open UDP 24454".

Relay through a VPS: the relay checks accounts (Velocity), Play at home, locked settings

Apps
Hopper Server only. Desktop parity comes later.
Before
The relay passed players straight to the server at home. The server checked accounts itself and saw every player as coming from 127.0.0.1.
After
The relay setup also installs Velocity on the VPS (its own user, service and pinned Java and jar), which checks accounts and passes each player's real address. On a server, "Use the relay" adds FabricProxy-Lite (with Fabric API if missing) or Proxy Compatible Forge, writes its config, changes four server settings, records each in History and asks before restarting. "Stop using the relay" puts all of it back byte for byte. If the relay goes down, the red card offers "Play at home until it's back", and "Go back to the relay" once it is up. The four settings are locked in Settings while on the relay, with the reason and a link.

14 Nov 2026

Adding a mod to a paired instance offers to put it on the server too

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server's web page adds mods straight to a server and has no instance-to-server pairing, so the gap doesn't exist there.
Before
Adding a mod (and its dependencies) to an instance paired with a server only wrote into the instance. Group sync no longer deletes mod files the modpack comes with

Apps
Hopper desktop.

Hopper 0.72.3 · Hopper Server 0.67.0

14 Nov 2026

Start-up no longer takes 10-50 seconds after an update

Apps
Hopper desktop. The cache code is in the shared library, so Hopper Server gets the same fix; its start-up was not timed.
Before
The first start after every update opened every mod file and read every config again before the window showed (10 to 50 s on real data). Later starts took 0.3 s.
After
The caches survive an update, so a start after an update costs about the same as any other start. Modpacks that ship extra mod files are checked once per pack version instead of on every start. Hopper's log notes any start-up step that takes longer than half a second. This version still does the slow read once, then it stops happening on updates.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/stampcache.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/deps.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/modpack.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/instances.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/time.rs
  • src/gpui/store.rs
partly checked
  • No screenshots: this is start-up timing, not a visual change. Measured on a test PC from the app's log with a scratch data folder and three throwaway instances (502 mod files each): the next start took 79 ms instead of 23 to 24 s, and a start after an update 277 ms. The first start of this build is slow once.
  • Tests passed: Hopper core 1356 and Hopper desktop 87 (new: the cache survives an app update but not a format change, a pack file checked once is not read again, a pack with no known file list is looked at again later). One Hopper Server test about the built web page failed because the test copy had no built web page (unrelated).
  • Not measured: real instances (not touched), a Hopper Server start, a second PC. Not moved off the window path: the pack check changes instance records the first draw reads. Left alone: the logo and author read can still pause a first start after a reboot.

Hopper Server: nodes and moves hardened

Apps
Hopper Server only. Nothing in this change alters the desktop.
Before
A user or token limited to one server could read or cancel other servers' tasks. Lowering a node's level didn't stop a running move. A node could be updated or removed in the middle of a move. The setup code went into the log and journal, the Linux data folder was readable by every user, and AI assistant (MCP) audit rows said "local".
After
Tasks are scoped to the server they belong to. Every move step re-reads your level on the node, so lowering it stops the move cleanly and leaves nothing behind. Updating a node is refused (409) during a move, and removing a node ends its moves. An older node is refused before a move starts. The setup code shows on the console and in setup-code.txt only, and logs are redacted. On Linux the data folder is private to the panel's account. MCP audit rows carry the caller's address. The guide has a new section, "Nodes: servers on other machines".
  • crates/hopperd/src/security.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/pack_api.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/relay.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/moves.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/main.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/applog.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/mcp.rs
  • crates/hopperd/README.md
partly checked
  • No screenshots: no visible change. Checked live on this PC with a scratch main panel and a scratch node: the setup code is in no log, a second join with the same key is refused, a token scoped to one server could not read or cancel another's tasks (403), and during a 300 MB move the node's update was refused (409) and lowering its level stopped the move with nothing left behind.
  • Tests: all passed (Hopper Server 315, Hopper core 1353, web page 55); ten new tests for scoping, audit, refused moves, level changes and the private folder (that one runs on Unix only).
  • Not checked: Linux (the private folder and the journal), a cut-link 1 GB run and node-to-node moves (a checklist is in the multi-panel design notes).

Hopper 0.72.2 · Hopper Server 0.66.0

14 Nov 2026

Hopper Server: move or copy a server to another machine

Apps
Hopper Server (web page and API). Hopper desktop only shows these moves in a server's History, without Undo; its own Move or copy button is not built yet.
Before
A server could only live where it was made. Moving it to a node meant copying its folders by hand.
After
In a server's "..." menu (or its row menu), "Move or copy to..." opens one dialog: where to (this panel or one of your nodes), Move or Copy, and "Bring backups" (off unless you tick it). Before anything is sent it checks that the server is stopped, that there is disk space, the port, Java and the loader on the other side, and each check shows as a tick, a warning or a cross. Progress shows in the same box. If the connection drops, it waits, and if it doesn't come back it pauses with a Resume button and sends only what is missing. If something goes wrong, nothing is left on the other machine. After a move the old copy is kept, stopped and marked "Moved to <node>": it never starts by itself (not by a schedule, wake-on-join or crash restart, and the Start button is off). "Keep using this copy" lets you use it here again; "Delete the old copy..." asks you to tick a box first and puts it in the servers folder's bin for 30 days. Both machines record the move in the server's History, and the audit log says who did it.

Groups: a loader update reaches everyone following the pack

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server has no group sync of instances.
Before
When one person in a group moved the pack's loader to a newer version (for example NeoForge 21.1.200 to 21.1.256), people already following the pack kept the old one.
After
The loader is synced like the mods: followers move to the new version too, and the task says who updated it ("Sam updated NeoForge to 21.1.256"; the pack row says "Sam updated the loader"). If the game is running, the new loader is installed once it closes. Two people changing it end on the later change everywhere, and nobody's copy is set back by an older one. Only the same loader is followed: if someone switches to a different one (NeoForge to Fabric), the pack shows "Alex switched this pack to Fabric. Install it again from their share code to follow."

Hopper desktop (the fix is in the shared library, so Hopper Server's mod checks get it too).

After
The file is the pack's, named "CC: Tweaked", with no warning. A copy you added yourself gets a quiet blue note: "CC: Tweaked is in twice. Only 1.120.2 loads; the other copy does nothing.", with a "Remove other copy" button. Fabric and Quilt keep the warning, because they really won't start. Existing installs are corrected the next time Hopper opens them (when the pack file is still cached).
