Hopper 0.72.0 · Hopper Server 0.65.0

14 Nov 2026

Hopper saves its own log, with buttons to open or copy it

Apps
Hopper desktop. Hopper Server already keeps its own log file (hopperd.log); it is not changed here.
Before
Hopper's own log only went to the console. The installed app has no console, so a player had nothing to send with a bug report.
After
Every run writes a log in the Hopper Data folder, under logs, and the last 5 runs are kept (one run stops writing at 10 MB). Settings › Advanced has a "Hopper's log" row with "Open logs folder" and "Copy log". Copy log puts the log on the clipboard (the whole file when it is small, otherwise the last 2,000 lines) and shows "Log copied". Keys, tokens, passwords, share codes and your Windows user name are hidden in each line before it is written.

Hopper Server: give people and tokens a whole node, and act on many servers across nodes

Apps
Hopper Server (web page, API, command line and AI assistants). Hopper desktop has no code change: it was checked with a token limited to one node, and it has no multi-select on its Servers page, so no bulk actions there.
Before
A person's or API token's server list could only name single servers. Bulk actions only covered this panel and other panels, not a panel's nodes. The audit log had no way to show one node. A node's limit could only be changed by leaving and joining again. In a node's menu, Pause node and Update did nothing.
After
Users and API tokens can be given "All on <node>": every server on that node, including ones made there later, and nothing else. It only narrows what they reach; their role and the node's own limit still apply. Servers can be picked in the list, or with "Select all on <node>" in a node's menu, and started, stopped, restarted or sent a command in one go; one confirmation lists what happens per panel and node, and servers you can't touch are listed as skipped with why. Security › Audit log has a tab per node, and every line names its node. The main's limit on a node can be lowered from Settings › Nodes, from the API, or on the node with hopperd node ceiling (only lowered; raising it means joining again). AI assistants connected to the panel see and act on node servers with the same checks. A node that leaves on its own is taken out of every person's and token's list, a failed node update still says so after the main restarts, and a node started with --home finds its node file again.

Hopper 0.71.1 · Hopper Server 0.64.1

14 Nov 2026

Keep everyone in step: no extra copies of a group pack

Apps
Hopper desktop only (Hopper Server has no friends sync).
Before
A group pack kept in step was only marked as followed after its install finished. If the install failed, was cancelled, or Hopper was closed first, the next start made another copy (one player ended up with three copies of the same pack, only one in sync).
After
The follow is recorded as soon as the copy exists. An unfinished install shows its update as waiting on that same copy and finishes into it (retry, Try again, or the next start). No new copy is made. A cancelled install stays as a failed update ("Cancelled") that Hopper tries again later, and Hopper never deletes the copy. If you already have extra copies, keep the one that gets the group's updates and delete the others.

  • crates/hopper-core/src/groupsync.rs

  • Found, not fixed: if Hopper is closed in the few seconds after a pack's files are copied but before the follow is recorded, one unfollowed copy can still be left. Closing that gap needs the copy's id passed into the pack install.

Hopper Server: a paired panel that freezes now shows as down in the server list

Apps
Hopper Server web page only. Hopper desktop asks panels with time limits and has no live connection that can hang, so it does not have this hole.
Before
A paired panel that hung, or whose network dropped without a word, stayed green with its servers listed for as long as you looked (64 s and 72 s in two runs). The page waited for the browser to say the live connection closed, and with a frozen panel that never comes.
After
The page notices when a panel goes quiet. About 9 seconds after the freeze the group says "Can't reach <panel> · Reconnecting…" with an amber light and its servers dimmed ("Seen just now"), at about 24 seconds it turns to Offline with a red light, and when the panel answers again it turns green by itself (10.5 s and 16.7 s in two runs). It is the same path a panel that was stopped cleanly already took.

Servers: turn a paired panel off, refresh the list, and retry a slow panel

Apps
Both. Hopper desktop (Settings › Servers and the Servers page) and the Hopper Server web page (the panel group menu and the server list).

Nodes: Hopper desktop shows each node's own state, makes servers on a node and updates nodes; Hopper Server updates nodes and streams big uploads

Apps
Both. Hopper desktop is new here. Hopper Server gets the node updates, the "Update needed" state, and the streaming uploads; the web page was already done and was checked again in a browser.
Before
A node's group in Hopper desktop used the panel's light, said nothing about the node and had no +. Nodes could only be updated from the web page. A node too far behind the main was not called out, and big uploads to a node's server (world files, mod jars) had to be copied by the main.
After
In Hopper desktop a node's group shows the node's own state like the web panel: a coloured light and a line under its name when it is offline, reconnecting, updating, failed to update or needs an update. The + on a node's group makes a new server on that node. A node that needs an update has "Update" in its group menu for people the panel lets update its nodes. In Hopper Server, Settings › Nodes has Update and Update all: nodes update one at a time and it stops at the first one that fails. A node too far behind the main shows "Update needed" with what to do and stays read-only until updated. Big uploads to a node's server go straight through the main panel, with no size limit and no copy kept on the main.

Hopper Server: server passwords are only shown to people who may change the server's files

Apps
Hopper Server only.
Before
Anyone who could see a server could read its RCON password and management server secret: from its settings, from the server.properties file, from saved-over copies or from backups.
After
Those passwords are only shown to people who may change the server's files. Viewers and Operators get a short message instead of that file, and zips and search leave it out.

Hopper Server 0.62.0

13 Nov 2026

Hopper Server nodes: run servers on other machines from one panel

Apps
Hopper Server only. The desktop talks to panels, not nodes, so there was nothing to match yet (it gets node servers in 0.70.0).
Before
A node could join a main panel and stay connected, but it served nothing, and Settings › Nodes was hidden.
After
In Settings › Nodes, make a join command and paste it on the other machine; it connects to this panel by itself, so it needs no open port. A node's servers show in the server list, under their own group, and open the same server pages: start, stop, console, files, backups and the rest. For each node you choose what this panel may do: Look, Operate or Manage, and the node refuses anything above it. Each node shows its CPU, memory and disk, and how much memory its servers are promised. Maintenance mode pauses a node: no new starts, and no offline alert while you work on it. There is an alert when a node goes offline and another when it's back. In New server you choose which machine to make it on.

Hopper 0.69.1 · Hopper Server 0.61.0

13 Nov 2026

Pairing with a Hopper Server is safer

Apps
Both. Hopper desktop and Hopper Server.
Before
Someone between your PC and the panel could fake the 6-digit pairing code, and the code showed up in the panel's log.
After
A machine in the middle can no longer fake the code; PCs that are already paired keep working. An older Hopper trying to pair is told to update Hopper first, and pairing with an older Hopper Server says to update Hopper Server first, instead of failing. The pairing code no longer appears in the panel's log.

Hopper Server 0.60.0

13 Nov 2026

Hopper Server: pick servers across panels and start, stop, restart or command them together

Apps
Hopper Server only. The desktop's server list has no selection or bulk actions yet, so the two are not in step.
Before
Each server had to be started, stopped or restarted on its own page, and the list had no filters.
After
A Select button in the list header turns on tick boxes. Pick servers in every panel's group (click, drag across, or Ctrl+A), then use Start, Stop, Restart or Command in the selection bar. One question, grouped by panel, says what each server gets or why it's skipped, then the same dialog shows each result: what worked, what failed and why, and what was skipped. Servers where you can only look are skipped and never sent anything, and each panel checks your role and keeps its own audit log. The list can be filtered by panel and by state (All, Running, Stopped, Crashed).

Hopper Server only. Multi-panel is a web page feature; the desktop has no added panels.

After
Downloads, pictures, text previews, backups, zip entries and mod uploads on another panel's server all go to that panel (one-use links for downloads and new tabs, token reads for pictures and text, uploads to its API). "Add a Hopper Server" can download the panel's certificate and explains how to trust it on Windows, Mac and Firefox, and the page says plainly when it can't reach a panel and why that may be the certificate.

Hopper 0.69.0 · Hopper Server 0.59.0

13 Nov 2026

Friend groups: share a mod's settings with your group (coming soon)

Apps
Hopper desktop. Hopper Server is not part of this. The sharing turns on as soon as the friends server is updated, so it is coming soon; this release only has the desktop side.
Before
A group pack shared the pack's mods but never anyone's settings, so everyone tuned configs by hand.
After
Coming soon: in an instance's Configs tab, turn on "Share these settings" for a mod. The settings you changed from the pack go first, then every later change, one setting at a time, to everyone in the group; friends who are offline catch up when they're back. Only the settings you change are sent, so each friend keeps their other settings. Passwords, tokens and files marked never-share stay on your PC. Nothing is written while the game runs: settings that arrive wait until you close it, show in History as "Owner changed AppleSkin settings" with each change old to new, and can be undone one by one. "Keep mine" stops taking a mod's settings. The group page lists the shared settings with a Stop button, and a mod's menu has "Stop sharing settings".

Hopper Server: sign in and work from another panel's page

Apps
Hopper Server only. This is the server side of using several panels from one page; the next releases build the page that uses it.
Before
A panel's web page could only talk to its own panel. Other pages got cross-site headers but had no way to sign in, open live updates or download files.
After

An unlisted page gets nothing usable.   • No "before" shots: the before is the existing peek with no tab (see the Comments card in 0.68.0).
  • Most checks used saved fixtures to spare GitHub's hourly limit; a live fetch on the final build, a real 403 or 429 from GitHub, zoom other than 100% and keyboard focus rings weren't checked.

Groups: GitHub's hourly limit shows once and waits for its reset

Apps
Hopper desktop (the shared library change also reaches Hopper Server).
Before
A friend in a group got a flood of error toasts when GitHub's hourly download limit ran out: every new change from any member restarted the count and raised a new toast, and Hopper retried every 2 to 15 s (about 240 tries an hour) against a limit that was already used up. Each GitHub mod in a pack cost one API call on every follow and every merge.
After
One notice: "GitHub is limiting downloads for now. Hopper tries again at HH:MM.", with an "Add a GitHub token" button when none is saved. Retries wait for GitHub's reset time. An install says once "N mods from GitHub aren't in yet: GitHub is limiting downloads until HH:MM. Update again after that." GitHub mods in a shared pack now download straight from the release page, so following a pack uses none of the limit (a pack of 10 GitHub mods went from 10 API calls per member to 0), and GitHub's answers are reused for a while so update checks use far less. The Settings row for the GitHub token is always shown, with a link to make one; it needs no permissions.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/sources/http.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/sources/mod.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/sources/github.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/recipe.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/sharecode.rs
  • src/gpui/store/friends.rs
  • src/gpui/app.rs
  • src/gpui/settings_page.rs
seen on screen
  • Two scratch copies of Hopper (a sharer and a member), a local friends service and a fake GitHub in "limited" mode, with one mod's download made to fail. The member followed the pack: 9 of 10 mods came in, one note, no toast. The sharer toggled a mod 4 times: the member merged 4 times with no toasts and 0 API calls.
  • Tests passed: hopper-core 1,268, including ones for GitHub's reset headers, pausing calls until the reset, unchanged answers coming from the cache, the token only going to GitHub's API, and ten GitHub mods needing no API calls.
partly checked
  • Not seen on screen: the "GitHub is limiting downloads…" notice with its token button. In the live run the follow and merges partly succeeded and fell into the single note, so the notice (the whole follow failing) never fired; a test covers it. The wording without a token also wasn't seen, because the scratch builds read the maintainer's saved token (never sent to the fake).
  • No "before" shots: the old build can't simulate the limit without using up the real one.

Server sync: a detailed task, and Hopper Server downloads public mods itself

Apps
Both. The Tasks row is in Hopper desktop; Hopper Server gets a new way to fetch a file by link. The web page has no sync task, so it didn't change.
Before
The sync row said only "Updating <server>" with a bar, every jar was uploaded from your PC (slow on a home connection), and the done row just said it finished.
After
The row says the step in plain words and "N of M files". A second line shows the current mod's icon and name with "done of total · speed · time left". Each public jar from Modrinth, CurseForge or GitHub is downloaded by Hopper Server itself and checked by its hash, falling back to upload if that fails or there is no link. The done row says what changed ("Added X, Y; updated Z; removed W · Hopper Server downloaded 3 files itself, 1 uploaded (3 MB)") and lists each failed file with its reason.

The same small pack took 20.8 s and 4.14 MB uploaded on the old panel, and 17.2 s and 3.20 MB (only the custom jar) on the new one. A real pack of 300 public jars would save almost the whole upload.

Config editor: typing counts as a change right away

Apps
Both. Hopper desktop's config editor and Hopper Server's web one had the same problem.
Before
Typing in a text or number field changed nothing until you left it. The footer stayed "No unsaved changes" and Save stayed off. Clicking empty space didn't leave the field.
After
Every valid keystroke updates the setting and the footer count and Save follow at once. A bad value shows its reason under the field and keeps the last good value. Click outside lets go of the field, Enter finishes it, and Escape (desktop) puts the setting back to what it was when the field opened.

Hopper 0.66.1 · Hopper Server 0.57.1

12 Nov 2026

Crash help: when a mod's addon breaks after that mod updated, the card names the addon and the update

Apps
Both. The same crash engine runs in Hopper desktop and Hopper Server; the web page was checked on a scratch panel.
Before
A crash with Vista: Advanced Player read like a startup failure ("failed to load"), blamed Vista as a suspect, offered "Update Vista: Advanced Player" when no newer version was out, and "While you last played" flagged other mods such as Connectivity for harmless warnings.
After
The card says "Vista: Advanced Player doesn't match Vista" and explains that Vista was updated today (5.6.5 to 5.6.8) while the addon was made for an older Vista. Step 1 turns the addon off; step 2 is to undo the Vista update (it may help). If the same problem comes back after going back, the card says so ("tried 5.6.8 and 5.6.5") and offers only turning the addon off. "Update X" is dropped when the last check found nothing newer, and a mod named only in an unrelated part of the log is no longer blamed.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/crashdetect.rs

Friend groups: catch up after being away, pack mods turn off, old versions stay gone

Apps
Hopper desktop (the friends service got one small field). Hopper Server has no group sync.
Before
A member who had been offline missed the other members' changes. A pack mod the sharer turned off stayed on for members even though the task said it changed, and a member passing a change on late could bring older mod versions back.
After
A member who comes back gets every change, in one batch per sender. Turning a mod off, on or out reaches everyone, including mods a pack lists by file name. A late copy can no longer undo a newer change: each mod's state carries a stamp of the change that made it. If your own unsent change was held for too long, yours wins and theirs to that mod is dropped. This needs the updated sync service for the stamps.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/groupsync.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/mailbox.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/onlyhere.rs
  • src/gpui/store/friends.rs
  • relay/hopper_mailbox.py
partly checked
  • No screenshot: nothing on screen changed apart from task notes.
  • Tests only: a simulated owner and two members with a fake relay and a restart (the returning member got all 4 changes in one batch and nothing was undone on the others), a late pass-on that used to bring an old file back, a first copy taking only dated changes, and pack mods named by file. hopper-core 1,237 passed and the relay's tests passed (84 OK).
  • Not tried on screen: a real run on two PCs or against the real friends service.

Updating a GitHub mod no longer leaves it in twice

Apps
Both. The fix is in the shared library, so Hopper desktop and Hopper Server get it together. Nothing visible changed.
Before
If a mod update finished while a group sync, a followed pack or "Sync with friends again" was changing the same instance, the older copy was saved over the update. The new jar stayed in the mods folder with no entry, an older version came back beside it, and History had no row for the file that was replaced. The real case: two History rows and the mod loaded twice.
After
Changes to an instance's mods now take turns. An update reads the instance again before it puts files in, and updating a GitHub or link mod moves any leftover unlisted jars of it to History. The result is one jar, one entry and one History row per update. If a mod changed again while its update downloaded, the update is left out with a note to check for updates again.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/instances.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/install.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/groupsync.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/recipe.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/onlyhere.rs
partly checked
  • No screenshot: nothing visible changed.
  • Four new tests cover an update from an older copy, a leftover jar going to History, an update for a file that changed meanwhile, and the lock. With the reload taken out two of them fail; with it back everything passes. The whole run passed (hopper-core 1,225, Hopper Server 144, desktop 82).
  • Not checked: a live race in the running app, or Hopper Server end to end. The real instance was only read.

The cause was a spacer written as "&emsp;" that the parser didn't know, which pushed the whole table to the plain Markdown view; SVG badges also draw again.

Friends: keep a mod "Only on this PC", and a "Just for me" toast for locked mods

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server has no friends sync, so nothing changes there.
Before

After

12 Nov 2026

Friends panel redesign: a rail of groups, drag a friend onto a group, and a status chip

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server has no Friends panel.
Before
The panel had Friends and Your packs tabs, and a group was something you opened from a list. There was no way to move people between groups quickly, and nothing in the title bar said whether the packs you follow were doing fine.
After
The tabs are gone. A rail on the left holds Everyone, one tile per group (invitations first, with a dot), a + for a new group and Packs at the foot. Drag a friend onto a group tile to invite them; Ctrl+drag adds them and keeps their old group, and the hint beside the pointer says what dropping does or why it can't. A toast with Undo follows. Dragging a member of your group onto the strip under the header takes them out, also with Undo. The ⋯ menus ("Add to group", "Move to", "Take out…") do the same from the keyboard. A chip next to "Friends" says how the packs you follow are doing: In sync, Syncing, After the game, Couldn't update or Needs your answer. Click it to open Packs, where a card says which pack and why. Group packs show who changed them last: "Mia turned off 2 mods · 3 h ago". A pack that needs your answer no longer also says "up to date".

  • The "Syncing" and "After the game" chips are short-lived and weren't caught on screen; they use the same code as the other three.
  • The "changed by" line counts mods ("turned off 2 mods") rather than naming one, because the settings keep marks by mod key, not name.
  • The Hopper Server web test that needs a built web page failed in this worktree for that reason only.

Followed and group packs update while the game runs

Apps
Hopper desktop (the on/off wait also applies to Hopper Server, below).
Before
While a friend played, their copy of a followed or group pack got no changes. The row said "changes come when the game closes", and the changes only went in after they quit. Turning a mod on or off by hand while the game held its file could fail.
After
Changes go in while the game runs, the same way Update mods works, and the Mods tab says "Changed while the game runs: restart it to load the changes". A mod the game holds open is switched once the game closes. The Mods tab says how many are waiting ("1 mod the game has open will be turned off when it closes.") and nothing fails. On a Hopper Server, turning mods on or off while the server runs waits the same way and is finished before the next start.
  • src/gpui/store/friends.rs
  • src/gpui/friends.rs
  • src/gpui/addons_extra.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/locked.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/install.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/sharecode.rs
seen on screen
  • Two scratch copies of Hopper and a local friends service. A fake game held a mod's file open on the follower's PC: the owner turned AppleSkin off and added Mouse Tweaks, and within about 15 s the follower had Mouse Tweaks and the banner. After the fake game stopped, the AppleSkin jar became .jar.disabled and the Mods tab showed it off.
  • Manual switch: clicked off, back on and off again with the file held; each state matched the notice. The notice box lines up with the list header (0 px) and its text reads 14.7:1.
  • Tests passed: hopper-core 1,193 (including two new ones for the waiting switch), desktop 79.
partly checked
  • Not checked: group packs kept both ways (same code path, not run live), a follow that updates the modpack version while playing, a real Minecraft launch, the light theme, and dragging across switches over a held jar.
  • Hopper Server's waiting on/off was covered by tests only.

12 Nov 2026

Group packs: everyone's changes reach everyone, and the mod says who

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server has no friends or groups. Everyone in a group needs this version for changes to go both ways.
Before
In a group, only the pack's owner sent changes. A member turning a mod off, adding, removing or updating one changed only their own copy.
After
With "Keep my packs the same as the group" on, any member's change to a group pack reaches every member in a few seconds. If two people change the same mod, the latest change wins. On the Mods tab a mod changed by someone else carries a chip on the tag line: "Turned off by Mia", "Added by Sam", "Updated by Alex". Your own changes show no chip. The group's help text now says changes go both ways.

Following a friend's pack: changes arrive by themselves, even after a hiccup

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server has no Friends follow.
Before
If applying a friend's change failed once (a file held open, a bad connection, the game holding the mods), it was never tried again. The row showed an Update button and the error sat only in Tasks. Restarting didn't help.
After
Every followed pack with a newer version applies by itself: when Hopper starts, when the change arrives, after a failure (it tries again after 2, 2, 3, 5 and 10 s, then every 15 s) and when the game closes. The row says what is happening in plain words. An Update button only appears for mods from GitHub or other links that need your OK.

Hopper 0.63.2 · Hopper Server 0.55.1

11 Nov 2026

Syncing to a Hopper Server no longer hangs on a bad connection, and the Tasks bar shows the upload

Apps
Both. Hopper desktop sends the files; Hopper Server takes them in pieces and now logs why it refused one. The web page only got counts that read right.
Before
A sync to a remote Hopper Server sat at "Syncing 1 files" forever when a connection dropped mid-upload: the panel refused the piece with a plain-text error nobody logged, and the PC waited up to an hour for a reply. Adding mods to a server's pack sent each jar in one go, with one try.
After
Every piece has send and reply limits and up to 5 tries with a pause between them, and sending a piece twice is safe. If part of an upload fails, Hopper picks up where it left off; after a few failed tries it stops and says why in plain words. Adding mods uses the same resumable upload. The Tasks bar follows the bytes sent, and counts read right everywhere: "Syncing 1 file", "1 mod copied".

seen on screen

  • Live runs: a lost reply plus a cut piece recovered in 6 s; every piece cut ended in a plain error in 30 s; a stalled piece carried on after about a minute; a jar added with a lost reply was added once.
  • Tests passed: hopper-core 1,173, Hopper Server 143.
partly checked
  • The bar steps once per 8 MB piece, so on a very slow link it holds about 12 s per step.

11 Nov 2026

Following a friend's pack: changes keep coming through, and removals follow too

Apps
Hopper desktop.

Server packs made on Windows now start on Linux and macOS

Apps
Both. Hopper desktop and Hopper Server read the start command with the same code.
Before
On Linux, starting a NeoForge or Forge pack made on Windows ran the Windows arguments file. That file lists the mods with semicolons, so Java stopped with "Could not find or load main class cpw.mods.bootstraplauncher.BootstrapLauncher".
After
On Linux and macOS the pack runs its unix arguments file instead, and on Windows a pack that names the unix file gets the Windows one. If the other file is missing, the named one is used as before. The pack's own run and start scripts are left exactly as written, so they still work on the PC they came from.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/hosting/script.rs
partly checked
  • Tests cover both file choices for NeoForge and Forge, a pack where the other file is missing, other arguments staying put, and the start command picking the right file. The full hopper-core run passed.
  • Not checked: a real Linux start of a pack. That needs a Linux machine; the choice is one function with both outcomes tested.
  • There is no screenshot: nothing on screen changes on Windows.

Hopper Server updates itself on Linux

Apps
Hopper Server. Hopper desktop has no server self-update.
Before
On Linux the Update button and automatic updates were off: the panel said it updates itself on Windows only.
After
Linux panels on x64 and arm64 update like Windows ones: download, restart into the new version, and roll back from Other versions. It waits only while a server started by an older version is still running. Each download is checked against the SHA-256 fingerprint GitHub lists for that file, not just its size; a file that does not match is refused with a plain message and nothing is installed. Linux also checks that the file is a program for its own kind of machine.

  • crates/hopperd/Cargo.toml
partly checked

  • Not checked: anything on Linux. The Linux-only parts (file permissions, folder sync, starting the new file) were not even compiled here, and no Linux panel was run.
  • The only visible change is the note on the update card, and there is no Linux panel here to show it.

Hopper Server on Linux reconnects to its servers after a restart

Apps
Hopper Server on Linux. Hopper desktop uses the same process code but only runs on Windows, and Windows behaves as before.
Before
After a Linux panel restarted (an update, a crash or a service restart), servers that were running became orphans: no console, no commands, no stop. A Linux update therefore had to wait for every server to stop.
After
The restarted panel connects to them again, like Windows: console from the top, commands, stop and kill. Updates can go ahead while servers run. Only servers started by an older panel still hold an update back and offer "Stop servers and update" or "Wait for them to stop"; those servers need one stop and start before the panel can take them over.

Hopper Server: other web pages can use a panel, and the panel's own page is locked down

Apps
Hopper Server only. Hopper desktop has no web page and no cross-site access.
Before
The panel's web page had no Content-Security-Policy, and no other web page could call a panel's API from a browser.
After
The web page now follows a strict security policy: scripts only from the panel's own files. In Settings > Access & devices the owner can list other web pages, by their exact address, that may use this panel; those pages still have to sign in with a token. A switch, off by default, decides whether they may also manage users, devices, tokens, two-factor and the certificate. The list and the switch can only be changed from the panel's own page.

Mods tab opens faster and scrolls with less work on big packs

Apps
Hopper desktop. Hopper Server's web Mods table already counted from the list it was sent.

Mod pages: odd descriptions and file names can't close Hopper, and links, italics and strikethrough show right

Apps
Both. The desktop draws descriptions; Hopper Server's web UI uses the same parser, and strikethrough needed one small change there.
Before
On Modrinth, reference links showed as [text][16], <https://…> links vanished, _italic_ and ~~strike~~ showed their marks, and a < before a number ate the rest of the sentence. A Files tab on a mod with a non-ASCII file name, or a description ending in <, could close the app.
After
These all look like they do on the website. The crash paths are fixed and covered by tests: non-ASCII file names, a description that ends in <, a GIF claiming 65,535 x 65,535 pixels (now capped at 4,096), huge width and height numbers, and deeply nested or very long markup (30,000 escaped angle brackets in a row used to take 9 s; it now takes 0.17 s).

Mod icons and downloads no longer hang when the network drops a connection

Apps
Both. Hopper Server uses the same download code; it was not run.
Before
When a connection to a mod site was lost, the request waited the whole connect limit (10 s for the API, 30 s for downloads, or Windows' ~21 s) and then failed. A failed icon stayed failed until Hopper restarted.
After
A connection that gets no answer is dropped after 2.5 s and a new one opened, then after 3, 4 and 6 s, all inside the original limit. Fetching Sodium's icon 40 times from the two addresses that lose connections: 11-18 of 40 failed before, 2 of 40 after; the good ones arrived in 2.7-9.7 s. The API's connect limit went from 10 to 20 s so it gets five tries.

Friends: one panel to share packs with friends and groups, and play the same ones

Apps
Hopper desktop only. Hopper Server has no friends or sharing screens yet, so there is nothing to keep in step there.
Before
Friends and sharing lived in an older friends window with a "Paired" chip, a "From anywhere" option and its own invite countdown. A pack could only be shared with everyone at once. The side panel could draw only its top ~300 px after a popover opened, so the panel looked cut in half.
After
A Friends button in the title bar (with a dot when there is news) opens a full-height panel beside the page, with Friends and Your packs tabs. Each pack is shared per friend or per group from a popover under its Share button. Friends' packs can be followed into an instance you pick, or into a new one. Groups can be created, invited to, joined, kept in step, renamed, have friends added or taken out, and left or deleted. A share code can also carry a one-time "get their changes" part, and installing from one lets you get the sender's changes too. The panel is the same one the mod peek uses, so it keeps one width and one place.

Hopper 0.60.5 · Hopper Server 0.53.2

10 Nov 2026

Browse: opening some packs no longer closes Hopper, and descriptions show their pictures instead of raw code

Apps
Both: Hopper desktop and the Hopper Server web UI. The version sort and the description renderer are shared, and Hopper Server's update check had the same sort problem.
Before
Opening "Matcha Block Overlays" (a Modrinth resource pack) from Browse closed Hopper at once. Its version list mixes releases (26.3, 1.21.10) with snapshots (22w42a, 24w14potato), and the version sort could go round in a circle, which Rust treats as a fatal error. Fresh Animations' description began with a grey code box full of raw image, centre and details markup, because a single line of code was read as the start of a code block.
After
Versions always sort in one order, newest first (26.3, 26.2, 26.1.2 ...), and the peek opens. Hopper Server's own update check got the same fix (1.0 against 1.0.0). In a description, that single line now shows as small inline code, then the Early Access images, a rule, the banner image and the text; the author's real code block still shows as a code box.

Hopper 0.59.0 · Hopper Server 0.53.1

9 Nov 2026

Big mod lists use far less memory

Apps
Hopper desktop. The Hopper Server web UI's pictures are the browser's own.
Before
Every picture Hopper showed was decoded at full size and kept until Hopper closed: mod icons, instance banners, Browse cards, screenshots and server art. Scrolling a 1,188-mod list ended at about 646 MB working set and 749 MB private.
After
Pictures are decoded no wider than they are shown and kept to a 96 MB budget, dropping the least recently used. The image viewer frees its picture when it closes. The same scroll ends at about 454 MB working set and 548 MB private. Jar scans at start-up use 3 threads instead of 8, and freed memory goes back to Windows: the start-up peak on a copy of a real pack fell from about 529 MB to 433 MB. The same pictures look the same (mean pixel difference 0.21 to 0.36 of 255 in the crops).

Hopper 0.58.0 · Hopper Server 0.53.0

8 Nov 2026

Changes in the files: see what changed in a mod version when its author wrote no changelog

Apps
Both: Hopper desktop and the Hopper Server web UI. For a server on a Hopper Server, the desktop does not show this yet: it has no screen for a remote server's mod versions.
Before
A mod version with no changelog just said "No changelog for this version."
After
Off by default. The first version without a changelog in a list offers: "The author didn't write a changelog. Hopper can compare the files to show what changed." with Turn on and Not now. With it on, Hopper compares the old and the new file and lists what visibly changed: new blocks and items, new settings with the author's own description, added languages, changed dependencies, new loot tables and textures. It says plainly that this comes from the files, not the author: "Scanned from the jar files by Hopper, not by the author." If only the mod's code changed it says so, and when only the version details changed it says the files have the same content. It may download one of the two files once, then keeps only a small result. You can turn it on from the notice or in Settings > Mod sources. On Hopper Server only the owner can change the setting; everyone signed in sees the result.

Servers that sleep: modded players can wait on the connecting screen (experimental)

Apps
Hopper Server: a new "Modded players" row in the server's Sleep settings. The same row exists in the desktop Sleep settings for a server on a Hopper Server.
Before
A NeoForge or Forge player who joined a sleeping modded server started it, but was then turned away with a "join again" message.
After
New setting "Modded players" with "Join again message" (the default, as before) and "Wait (experimental)". With Wait, a modded player stays on Minecraft's normal "Connecting to the server..." screen while the server starts and joins by themselves when it is up. There is no progress bar on that screen. Hopper keeps the connection alive, gives up after 10 minutes, and shows the usual "couldn't start" message if the start fails. One connection per player and server, and no more than 16 per server. It only applies when sleep and wake-on-join are on, and it does not change who may wake a server. Vanilla players still wait on the small lobby island.
  • crates/hopperd/src/hold.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/router.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/limbo.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/hosting/router.rs

partly checked
  • Tests for the wait itself: what is sent to the player, one request at a time, handing over only after the last answer, the player leaving, a failed start, the time limit, a player that never answers, junk and floods dropped, and extra bytes kept. The whole test run passed.
  • Tried with a scripted client against a scratch server with a fake backend: a modded-marked player was held about a minute while the fake server started, then reached the fake backend with the original login. Failure, leaving, and the same name joining twice behaved as intended.
  • Tried with a real NeoForge 1.21.1 client on that fake server: the first join was turned away (its login carried no Forge marker), the second was held 34 s on the connecting screen and reached the fake backend. After that run the hold was changed so that on a server already known to be modded everyone is held, with no marker needed. That change has not been tried again with a real client.
  • Still unproven: the handoff to a real NeoForge server and its mod check. The fake backend only sent a disconnect message. Also not tried: a real Hopper Server, Fabric players, and many connections at once.
  • Seen on screen: the web Sleep card in a scratch panel, dark and light. The new row lines up with the others and is one line high like its siblings at 100 %. At 125 % in a narrow window it wraps like the existing "While it starts" row, so it does not fit on one line there. Hopper 0.57.0 · Hopper Server 0.52.0

8 Nov 2026

Mod changelogs look the same everywhere, and no false "Same notes as"

Apps

Before

Server Settings > Sleep: the wake-on-join options are always visible

Apps
Both: the Hopper Server web UI and the Sleep card in Hopper desktop for a server on a Hopper Server.
Before

After

Hopper 0.56.1 · Hopper Server 0.51.1

8 Nov 2026

Server Mods tab: the list and counts follow the panel; the Updates icon no longer leaves a gap

Apps
Both: Hopper desktop (the Mods tab of a server on a Hopper Server) and Hopper Server (the list it sends to the desktop). The wide gap was desktop only.
Before
A server on a Hopper Server showed its mods as the panel had last written them down. Jars swapped on disk (by a sync, SFTP or a job) stayed listed under their old file with the old update offer ("Update all (16)", "All 1012"), and jars the panel did not track were left out of "All". With 10 or more updates the Updates icon in the filter row left a wide empty gap before the next icon.
After
The list drops mods whose jar is gone, as the panel's own table does. It asks the panel for update offers again when the set of jars changes. "All" counts the jars the panel does not track too. The Updates icon is only as wide as its icon and number, with 1, 16, 120 or 1000 updates.
  • crates/hopperd/src/parity_mods.rs
  • src/gpui/store/server_mods.rs
  • src/gpui/addons.rs
  • src/gpui/widgets.rs
partly checked
  • Tests for the icon width growing with the number of digits, for dropping list entries whose jar is gone, and for telling a swapped jar from a switched one.
  • Seen on screen in a test build paired to a scratch Hopper Server with a throwaway server of fake jars: the gap measured 94 px with 16 updates before and 8 px at 1, 16, 120 and 1000 updates after, in the dark and light themes at 100 % and 125 % zoom. After one jar was swapped and one added, the counts followed the panel within ten seconds without a click.
  • Not checked: a real server that is starting, the real panel, and the web UI's Mods tab on screen (the web UI already sizes the icon by digits). The 120 and 1000 update cases were not measured before the fix.
  • A failed refresh keeps the old list without a sign. The panel's own record still holds entries for swapped jars until something rewrites it; only the desktop list and the web table hide them.

Updates and syncs replace the old jar, turned-off ones too

Apps
Both: the rule is shared, so Hopper desktop and Hopper Server behave the same, and a remote server gets the same steps over its API.
Before
A sync that found a mod turned off on the server skipped it, so the server never got the new version. Earlier builds put the new jar beside the old turned-off one (the "Old copy" chip). Updating a pack or a mod replaced only the first copy it found, so a mod that already had two copies kept the other one.
After
A sync puts the newer file on the server turned on or off like the copy it replaces (off stays off) and removes the old file. Updating a pack or a single mod replaces every copy of that mod, turned on or off. The new file takes the state of the copy that was on. No older copy is left beside the new one. Mods you added yourself are left alone.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/install.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/modpack.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/rigsync.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/sync.rs
partly checked
  • Tests in throwaway folders: a pack update with the mod on, off, doubled with one on and one off, and doubled with both off; a pack that drops a mod; your own mod untouched; and syncs where the mod is off or on at the server, new, server-only, or already the same file. Each ended with one file per mod in the right state.
  • No screenshots: there is no visible change except that the sync row now reads as an Update instead of "kept off". Not screenshotted.
  • Not checked: a running app or panel, a real pack update from CurseForge, the remote (Hopper Server) path other than by reading the code, the real TNP-LL8 case, and undoing the new sync steps.
  • Old copies that already exist keep their "Old copy" chip; nothing is deleted at start-up. If a server has several turned-off copies of one mod, a sync replaces one and the rest still show the chip. If turning the new jar off fails after the copy, the new jar stays on.

Sync says when a mod's source did not go along to the panel

Apps
Both: Hopper desktop shows a line and a button after a sync; the Hopper Server web UI shows a hint in the Mods tab.
Before
When the panel had "mods from links" turned off, mods synced from GitHub or a link showed as "Unknown" on the panel with no word why.
After
After a sync the desktop says, for example, "9 mods' sources didn't go along: this panel has mods from links turned off." with a button that opens the panel's Settings > Mod sources. In the panel's Mods tab, the Unknown view shows a hint that the sources may not have come along, with a link to that setting, while the setting is off. The hint can be dismissed. Turning the setting on makes it go away. The button only opens the page; it does not turn the setting on.
  • crates/hopper-core/src/sources/carry.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/rigsync.rs
  • crates/hopper-core/src/hopperd.rs
  • src/gpui/rigsync.rs
  • src/gpui/side_card.rs
  • crates/hopperd/ui/src/Mods.svelte
  • crates/hopperd/ui/src/lib/sourcehint.js
partly checked
  • Tests that the report says why sources stayed behind, and for when the web hint shows.
  • Seen on screen: the web hint on a scratch Hopper Server with the setting off and three unknown mods. It shows only in the Unknown view, the link opens the Mod sources settings, and turning the setting on removes it. In the light and dark themes at normal and 125 % zoom (a CSS zoom, not the browser's own) the text was not clipped, the sides were even and the text and link were readable.
  • Not checked: the desktop line and its button have never been seen on screen. Pairing a scratch desktop would have put the panel token in Windows Credential Manager, so it was not done. The code builds and the wording is covered by a test.
  • The panel cannot know a desktop instance's sources, so its hint says "may". Panels that do not report this, and local-folder servers without a record, stay silent as before.

Hopper 0.56.0 · Hopper Server 0.51.0

8 Nov 2026

Hopper Server: the owner can let a paired PC manage the panel

Apps
Both: Hopper Server (web UI and panel) and Hopper desktop (wording only).
Before
A PC paired through the approval flow was always a plain device. Deleting or removing a server was refused, and the desktop told even the owner to delete it in the panel.
After
When the owner approves a PC, they can tick "Let this PC manage the panel (delete servers, change users)". It is off by default. It can be switched on or off later for each device in Settings > Access & devices. A PC with it on gets the normal delete dialog on the desktop. A PC without it is told what to do: allow the PC in the panel's Access & devices settings, or delete the server there. Only the owner can change this. A device cannot give it to itself, approve other PCs or remove devices. Every approval and switch is written to the audit log. Devices already paired stay off.
  • crates/hopperd/src/auth.rs
  • crates/hopperd/src/api.rs

  • Seen on screen on a scratch panel with throwaway servers: the web approval and device list, and both desktop messages. The rules that a manager or device cannot grant it were run against the scratch panel and refused every time. Delete was never pressed.
  • Centring of the tick box and the alignment of the switches were measured in the dark theme.
  • Not checked: a real manager user trying to grant it, the light theme, phone width, and an older desktop against the new panel (it still shows the old message until updated).

Deleting a server: one checkbox, and a clear message when something holds the folder

Apps
Both: Hopper Server (web UI and panel) and Hopper desktop (the dialog and the delete itself).
Before
Deleting a server could fail with "Access is denied" when the panel itself, or another program, held the folder. Deleting for good meant typing the server's name.
After
The panel lets go of its own hold on the folder first and tries the move again a few times. If a program still holds it, the message names that program. A "Delete permanently (can't be undone)" checkbox replaces the typed name: ticked, the dialog says the folder is deleted for good and the button reads "Delete permanently". A setting in Settings > Panel folder chooses which way the dialog starts. If the move fails, nothing is changed, and kept worlds are put back.

    • Seen on screen in a test build with a throwaway instance (counts faked): the collapsed badge at 1, 99, 175, 1234 and 12345, the expanded rail, and the toolbar with Update all at 12345. The web version was seen as a test page with the real stylesheet, not in the live panel.
    • Centring was measured on screenshots and found one pixel low at first; it was fixed after this release (see 0.53.6 and 0.55.0).
    • Not checked: the live Hopper Server app, task and History badges at large values, Update all at four or more digits, narrow windows, and real instances.
    • The History badge still stops at 99+.

    Hopper 0.53.2 · Hopper Server 0.48.1

    7 Nov 2026

    Sync tab: the comparison does its independent work at the same time

    Apps
    Hopper desktop. Hopper Server's mod scan reads turned-off mods faster too.
    Before
    Opening Sync did the pack scan, the checks on jars with no known side, the server scan, the last-crash read and the other reads one after another.
    After
    The pack and the server run together, the crash, config and marker reads run beside the comparison, and the jar checks and turned-off mod reads use several threads. The result is the same, in the same order. On a test set of 1,200 mods a side, checking the jars with no known side went from 952 ms to 170 ms.
    • crates/hopper-core/src/modrinth.rs
    • crates/hopper-core/src/rigsync.rs
    • src/gpui/rigsync.rs
    • crates/hopper-core/tests/sync_bench.rs
    partly checked
    • New test for listing turned-on and turned-off jars and noticing changes. A benchmark checks that the threaded checks give the same answer, in the same order, as one at a time.
    • Seen on screen in a test build with a throwaway pack and server: the Sync tab finished with the expected counts.
    • Not checked: real data (a 1,158-mod pack), spinning disks, remote links, the real Modrinth cost, and a before and after on screen (no old build). Scanning and comparing were already cheap; the gain is in the checks and reads that wait on disk or network.
    • A peer test hangs in a full parallel run, passes alone, and was not fixed here.

    Sync: one timing line for every comparison

    Apps
    Hopper desktop, plus one line in Hopper Server's own log for the remote read. No change to what you see.
    Before
    There was no way to see where Sync's comparison spends its time.
    After
    Each time the Sync tab compares, it adds one line to sync-timing.log in Hopper's data folder, with how long each step took (scans, server fetch, Modrinth lookups, diff). The line holds only counts and times, no mod or file names. Hopper Server writes a similar line, server time only, to its own log.
    • crates/hopper-core/src/synctiming.rs
    • crates/hopper-core/src/rigsync.rs
    • src/gpui/rigsync.rs
    partly checked
    • Seen as a line written on a throwaway pack with 1,160 jars a side: total about 1.5 s, most of it Modrinth lookups.
    • Nothing new on screen, so no screenshot.
    • Not checked: real data, and the remote server fetch line.

    Hopper 0.53.0 · Hopper Server 0.48.0

    7 Nov 2026

    Mods tab: the filters are a row of icons

    Apps
    Both: Hopper and Hopper Server.
    Before
    A wide row of text tabs with counts (All, Updates, Disabled, Archived, Client-only, Unknown), plus separate "Added by you" and "Config changed" chips, "Update all" and a separate check button, and a "Sort: ..." text button.
    After
    One row of icons. The picked filter opens to its name and count and "All" starts open; the others show a tooltip. "Added by you" and "Config changed" are in the row, tinted with a ring when on, and combine with any other filter. "Client-only" shows on server pages only. Update all and "Check for updates" are one split button (the check side says when it last ran), and Sort is an icon that shows its name only when it is not the default order. On narrow windows the search stays on the first row.

    • crates/hopperd/ui/src/app.css
    partly checked

    • Seen on screen in both apps on throwaway instances and servers with seeded data: default, Updates, Updates plus Added by you, Config changed, Disabled, Archived, Unknown, Client-only, the split button with its checking spinner, tooltips, keyboard focus ring, and the narrow layout.
    • Not checked: Archived and Unknown on the desktop, the check button's real "checked N min ago" text on a finished check, arrow keys on the desktop, resource-pack tabs, and a real instance or server with thousands of mods. There is no old-build screenshot for the web side (the before shots are the desktop ones).

    Hopper 0.52.3 · Hopper Server 0.47.3

    6 Nov 2026

    Servers: the Mods tab no longer marks nearly every mod "Added by you"

    Apps
    Both: Hopper and Hopper Server (one shared rule).
    Before
    A server made from a server pack showed about 1,000 of its 1,158 mods as "Added by you". The server list ignored the pack's own mod list; instances were right (249).
    After
    A server compares against the pack's mod list like an instance does. If that list isn't available, nothing is marked as yours, instead of everything.
    • crates/hopper-core/src/modpack.rs
    • crates/hopperd/src/mods_api.rs
    • crates/hopperd/src/parity_mods.rs
    • src/gpui/store/server_mods.rs
    partly checked
    • Tests: 4 new for the pack marking, and all of Hopper Server's tests pass.
    • Seen on screen on a test server (12 mods, pack lists 9): desktop and the web UI in a real browser, with the pack list and without it. The instance code path is untouched.
    • Not checked: the real TNP-LL8 server. It most likely hit "pack list not available" on the panel; that is unconfirmed. After updating, its count should drop from about 1,013.
    • Not checked: a before screenshot of the web UI (no old build to hand).

    Crash cards: a timing problem inside a library no longer blames the mod the loader names

    Apps
    Both: the desktop crash card and Hopper Server's crash card.
    Before
    A TNP-LL8 crash caused by a loading race inside Moonlight Lib showed Supplementaries as "Certain" with "Turn off Supplementaries". The same race had blamed Snowy Spirit the day before.
    After
    Moonlight Lib is "Likely": "start again, this is a timing problem inside Moonlight Lib and often goes away; if it keeps happening, update it". Supplementaries is "Possible", and there is no Turn off button.
    • crates/hopper-core/src/crashdetect.rs
    • crates/hopper-core/src/crashengine.rs
    • crates/hopperd/ui/src/ServerCrash.svelte
    • crates/hopper-core/tests/crash_corpus/
    • design/crash-detection.md
    partly checked
    • Tests: the new rule has fixtures from both real crashes (5 and 6 Nov) and a case where the error is inside the named mod's own code, which still blames that mod. The whole crash corpus passes and no older case changed its verdict.
    • Seen on screen on a test server in both apps.
    • Not checked: a real game-instance crash card, narrow layouts, and the "Why?" list. There is no web "before" shot.
    • Only crashes where the loader's mod has the error thrown from another mod's code are covered.
